## I. The Van Normans / 一　范·诺曼一家

===Original===
The old Van Norman mansion was the finest house in Mapleton. Well back from the road, it sat proudly among its finely kept lawns and gardens, as if with a dignified sense of its own importance, and its white, Colonial columns gleamed through the trees, like sentinels guarding the entrance to the stately hall.
All Mapleton was proud of the picturesque old place, and it was shown to visiting strangers with the same pride that the native villagers pointed out the Memorial Library and the new church.
More than a half-century old, the patrician white house seemed to glance coldly on the upstart cottages, whose inadequate pillars supported beetling second stories, and whose spacious, filigreed verandas left woefully small area for rooms inside the house.
The Van Norman mansion was not like that. It was a long rectangle, and each of its four stories was a series of commodious, well-shaped apartments.
And its owner, the beautiful Madeleine Van Norman, was the most envied as well as the most admired young woman in the town.
Magnificent Madeleine, as she was sometimes called, was of the haughty, imperious type which inspires admiration and respect rather than love. An orphan and an heiress, she had lived all of her twenty-two years of life in the old house, and since the death of her uncle, two years before, had continued as mistress of the place, ably assisted by a pleasant, motherly chaperon, a clever social secretary, and a corps of capable servants.

===Chinese===
古老的范·诺曼宅邸（Van Norman mansion）是梅普尔顿（Mapleton）最气派的宅子。它远离大路，傲然坐落于修剪齐整的草坪与花园之中，仿佛对自己的身份怀着一份庄严的自觉；那一排殖民风格的白柱透过树影熠熠生辉，宛如一列哨兵，守卫着通往庄严厅堂的入口。
整个梅普尔顿都为这座风景如画的老宅而自豪；本地人领着来访的外乡人前去参观时，那份得意劲儿，与他们指点纪念图书馆（Memorial Library）和新教堂时一般无二。
这座贵胄气派的白房子已有半个多世纪的历史，它似乎冷眼睥睨着那些暴发户式的小宅：那些房子柱子单薄，却硬撑起向外悬挑的第二层；镂空花饰的游廊宽大敞阔，屋内的房间却被挤得只剩可怜的一点地方。
范·诺曼宅邸却全然不是这样。它是一座规整的长方形建筑，上下四层，每一层都是一连串宽敞周正的房间。
而它的主人——美丽的马德琳·范·诺曼（Madeleine Van Norman），则是全镇最受艳羡、也最受仰慕的年轻女子。
人们有时称她“雍容华贵的马德琳”。她属于高傲专断的那一类美人，令人仰慕敬畏有加，却难得让人生出亲昵的爱恋。这位孤女兼女继承人，二十二年的岁月都是在老宅里度过的；两年前叔父去世后，她便继续执掌这个家，由一位和蔼可亲、慈母般的陪护女伴、一位干练的社交秘书，以及一众得力的仆人从旁辅佐，把一切打点得井井有条。

===Original===
The mansion itself and an income amply sufficient to maintain it were already legally her own, but by the terms of her uncle’s will she was soon to come into possession of the bulk of the great fortune he had left.
Madeleine was the only living descendant of old Richard Van Norman, save for one distant cousin, a young man of a scapegrace and ne’er-do-weel sort, who of late years had lived abroad.
This young man’s early life had been spent in Mapleton, but, his fiery temper having brought about a serious quarrel with his uncle, he had wisely concluded to take himself out of the way.
And yet Tom Willard was not of a quarrelsome disposition. His bad temper was of the impulsive sort, roused suddenly, and as quickly suppressed. Nor was it often in evidence. Good-natured, easygoing Tom would put up with his uncle’s criticism and faultfinding for weeks at a time, and then, perhaps goaded beyond endurance, he would fly into a rage and express himself in fluent if rather vigorous English.

===Chinese===
宅邸本身，连同一份足以应付其开销的丰厚进项，在法律上早已归她所有；而按照叔父遗嘱的条款，她不久还将继承他遗留的那笔巨额财产中的大头。
老理查德·范·诺曼（Richard Van Norman）在世的骨血，只剩马德琳一人——此外便只有一位远房表亲，一个浪荡败家的年轻人，近年来一直旅居国外。
这位年轻人的少年时代是在梅普尔顿度过的，但他火爆的脾气惹出了一场与叔父的严重争吵，此后他识趣地决定自己抽身走开。
话说回来，汤姆·威拉德（Tom Willard）并非好斗之性。他的坏脾气属于冲动一类，来得突然，压下去也同样快，而且并不常见诸颜色。生性和善、随和的汤姆能一连数周忍受叔父的指责挑剔，然后或许被刺激得忍无可忍，才勃然大怒，用一段流利而火气十足的言语一吐为快。

===Original===
For Richard Van Norman had been by no means an easy man to live with. And it was Tom’s general amiability that had made him the usual scapegoat for his uncle’s ill temper. Miss Madeleine would have none of it. Quite as dictatorial as the old man himself she allowed no interference with her own plans and no criticism of her own actions.
This had proved the right way to manage Mr. Van Norman, and he had always acceded to Madeleine’s requests or submitted to her decrees without objection, though there had never been any demonstration of affection between the two.
But demonstration was quite foreign to the nature of both uncle and niece, and in truth they were really fond of each other in their quiet, reserved way. Tom Willard was different. His affection was of the honest and outspoken sort, and he made friends easily, though he often lost them with equal rapidity.
On account, then, of his devotion to Madeleine, and his enmity toward young Tom Willard, Richard Van Norman had willed the old place to his niece, and had further directed that the whole of his large fortune should be unrestrictedly bestowed upon her on her wedding-day, or on her twenty-third birthday, should she reach that age unmarried. In event of her death before her marriage, and also before her twenty-third birthday, the whole estate would go to Tom Willard.

===Chinese===
理查德·范·诺曼本来就绝不是个容易相处的人，而汤姆素来温厚随和，这反倒使他成了叔父坏脾气的惯常出气筒。马德琳小姐却一点不买账。她同老头子一样说一不二，绝不容许任何人干涉自己的计划，也不容许别人对她的行事指摘半句。
事实证明，这才是应付范·诺曼先生的正确法子。他向来应允马德琳的请求，或者毫无异议地顺从她的决断，尽管两人之间从未有过什么亲昵的表示。
不过，亲昵的表示本就与叔侄二人的天性无缘；说实在的，他们是以各自恬静含蓄的方式真心关爱着彼此。汤姆·威拉德便不同了。他的感情直率坦诚，交朋友极容易，只是常常也同样飞快地失去朋友。
于是，出于对马德琳的钟爱和对年轻的汤姆·威拉德的厌恶，理查德·范·诺曼立下遗嘱，把老宅留给了侄女，并进一步载明：他的巨额财产将全部毫无限制地授予她——或在她成婚之日，或在她二十三岁生日那天，倘若届时她尚未出阁。而倘若她在成婚之前、又未及二十三岁生日便去世，全部产业则归汤姆·威拉德所有。

===Original===
It was with the greatest reluctance that Richard Van Norman decreed this, but a provision had to be made in case of Madeleine’s early death, and Willard was the only other natural heir. And now, at twenty-two, Madeleine was on the eve of marriage to Schuyler Carleton, a member of one of the oldest and best families of Mapleton.
The village gossips were pleased to commend this union, as Mr. Carleton was a man of irreproachable habits, and handsome enough to appear well beside the magnificent Madeleine.
He was not a rich man, but, as her marriage would bring her inheritance, they could rank among the millionaires of the day. Yet there were those who feared for the future happiness of this apparently ideal couple.
Mrs. Markham, who was both housekeeper and chaperon to her young charge, mourned in secret over the attitude of the betrothed pair.

===Chinese===
立下这样的条款，理查德·范·诺曼实在是万般不愿，但万一马德琳早逝，总得有个安排，而威拉德是除此之外唯一的血亲继承人。如今，二十二岁的马德琳即将与斯凯勒·卡尔顿（Schuyler Carleton）完婚——此人出身于梅普尔顿最古老、最显赫的世家之一。
镇上爱说闲话的人都乐于称道这桩婚事：卡尔顿先生品行无可指摘，相貌又足够英俊，站在雍容华贵的马德琳身旁毫不逊色。
他并不富有，不过她一成婚便可继承遗产，两人尽可跻身于当今的百万富翁之列。然而，也有人为这对表面上天造地设的璧人日后的幸福暗暗担忧。
马卡姆太太（Mrs. Markham）身兼年轻女主人家的管家与陪护女伴之职，她暗自为这对订婚男女相处的情态而伤神。

===Original===
“He adores her, I’m sure,” she said to herself, “but he is too courtly and polished in his manner. I’d rather he would impulsively caress her, or involuntarily call her by some endearing name than to be always so exquisitely deferential and polite. And Madeleine must love him, or why should she marry him? Yet she is so haughty and formal, she might be a very duchess instead of a young American girl. But that’s Madeleine all over. I’ve never seen her exhibit any real emotion over anything. Ah, well, I’m an old-fashioned fool. Doubtless, they’re cooing doves when alone together, but their high-bred notions won’t allow any sentiment shown before other people. But I almost wish she were going to marry Tom. He has sentiment and enthusiasm enough for two, and the relationship is so distant it’s not worth thinking about. Dear old Tom! He’s the only one who ever stirs Madeleine out of that dignified calm of hers.”
And that was true enough. Madeleine had inherited the Van Norman traits of dignity and reserve to such an extent that it was difficult for anyone to be a really close friend.
She had, too, a strange little air of preoccupation, and even when interested in a conversation would appear to look through or beyond her companion in a way that was discouraging to the average caller.
So Miss Van Norman was by no means a favorite with the Mapleton young people in a personal sense, but socially she was their leader, and to be on her invitation list was the highest aspiration of the village “climbers.”
And now that she was about to marry Schuyler Carleton, the event of the wedding was the only thing talked of, thought of, or dreamed of by Mapleton society.

===Chinese===
“他分明深爱着她，这一点我敢肯定，”她自忖道，“只是他的言谈举止未免太文雅、太讲究分寸了。我倒宁愿他会一时冲动地爱抚她，或是情不自禁地唤她一声昵称，也不愿他永远这般精致周到、恭恭敬敬。马德琳也必定爱他，不然她为什么要嫁给他呢？可她又那么高傲拘谨，活脱脱一位公爵夫人，哪里像个年轻的美国姑娘。不过，马德琳从头到脚就是这么副性子。我从没见她对任何事情流露过真切的情感。唉，我是个老派的傻瓜。无疑，他们俩单独相处时定是呢喃细语的一对鸽子，只是良好的教养不容他们在外人面前显露情肠。可我几乎巴不得她嫁的是汤姆。他的深情与热忱足够抵两个人，何况那点亲缘远得微不足道，根本不值一提。亲爱的老汤姆！天底下唯有他，能把马德琳从她那端庄的沉静里搅动起来。”
这话说得一点不假。马德琳把范·诺曼家端庄矜持的气质继承得太彻底，以致谁想同她做真正贴心的朋友都难。
她还带着一种莫名的心不在焉的神情，即便谈得饶有兴致，目光也仿佛穿过对方、望向更远处，叫寻常登门的客人好不气馁。
因此，就私交而论，范·诺曼小姐绝算不上梅普尔顿年轻人里的宠儿；但在社交场上，她却是他们的领袖，能列名于她的请客名单，正是镇上那些一心想“往上爬”的人家最大的心愿。
如今她即将嫁给斯凯勒·卡尔顿，这场婚礼便成了梅普尔顿社交界嘴上谈的、心里想的、梦里转的唯一大事。

===Original===
Madeleine, who always kept in touch with Tom Willard by correspondence, had written him of her approaching marriage, and he had responded by coming at once to America to attend the ceremony.
Relieved from the embarrassment of his uncle’s presence, Tom was his jovial self, and showed forth all the reprehensible attractiveness which so often belongs to the scapegrace nature. He sometimes quarreled with Madeleine over trifles, then, making up the next minute, he would caress and pet her with the privileged air of a relative.
He was glad to be back among the familiar scenes of Mapleton, and he went about the town renewing old acquaintances and making new ones, and charming all by his winning personality.
In less than a week he had more friends in the village than Schuyler Carleton had ever made.

===Chinese===
马德琳与汤姆·威拉德素来书信不断。她把自己即将成婚的消息写信告知了他，而他做出的回应，便是立刻渡海回美国参加婚礼。
再没有叔父在跟前令他拘束，汤姆恢复了乐天快活的本相，把浪荡子身上那种屡见不鲜、又实在不该招人喜欢的魅力展露无遗。他有时为点小事同马德琳拌嘴，转眼便和好，又带着亲戚特有的那份亲昵，对她又疼又宠。
重回梅普尔顿熟悉的一切中间，他满心欢喜，整日在镇上走动：旧雨重逢，新知日广，那讨人喜欢的性子把众人都迷住了。
不出一个星期，他在镇上交下的朋友，比斯凯勒·卡尔顿有生以来交到的总和还多。

===Original===
Carleton, though handsome and distinguished-looking, was absolutely without personal magnetism or charm, which traits were found in abundance in Tom Willard.
The friends of Schuyler Carleton attributed his reserved, almost repellent demeanor to shyness, and this was partly true. His acquaintances said it was indifference, and this again, was partly true. Then his enemies, of whom he had some, vowed that his cold, curt manner of speech was merely snobbishness, and this was not true at all.
His manner toward his fiancée was all that the most exacting could require in the matter of courtesy and punctilious politeness. He was markedly undemonstrative in public, and if this were true of his behavior when the two were alone, it was probably because Madeleine herself neither inspired nor desired terms or acts of endearment.
Tom’s attitude toward Madeleine angered Carleton extremely, but when he spoke to her on the subject he was gaily informed that the matter of cousinly affection was outside the jurisdiction of a fiancée.

===Chinese===
卡尔顿虽然相貌英俊、仪表出众，却全然没有个人的吸引力与魅力，而这些特质在汤姆·威拉德身上却多得漫出来。
斯凯勒·卡尔顿的朋友们把他那种矜持得近乎拒人千里的举止归因于羞怯——这话有几分真。熟人们说那是冷漠——这话也有几分真。至于他的对头们——他也确有几个——则一口咬定，他那冷淡生硬的说话做派不过是势利眼罢了——这话就全然不对了。
他对未婚妻的态度，论殷勤与礼数周全，即便最挑剔的人也无可指摘。他在人前显著地不露感情；倘若两人独处时依然如此，那多半是因为马德琳本人既不曾激发、也并不想要亲昵的言辞或举动。
汤姆待马德琳的这副态度令卡尔顿恼火极了；可他就此事向她提起时，却只得到一句轻快的回敬：表亲之间的情谊，不在未婚夫的管辖范围之内。

===Original===
Tom, on his part, was desperately in love with Madeleine, and had been for years. Repeatedly he had begged her to marry him, and she knew in her heart that his plea was prompted by his love for herself and not by any consideration of her fortune.
And yet, should she marry another, all hope of his uncle’s money would be forever lost to Tom Willard.
But prodigal and spendthrift that he was, if Tom felt any regret at his vanishing fortunes, he showed no sign of it. Save for sudden and often easily provoked bursts of temper, he was infectiously gay and merry, and was the life of the house party already gathered under Madeleine’s roof.
The fact that Tom was staying at the Van Norman house, which of course Carleton could not do, gave Willard an advantage over the prospective bridegroom, of which he was by no means unconscious. Partly to tease the imperturbable but jealous Carleton, and partly because of his own affection for his cousin, Tom devoted himself assiduously to Madeleine, especially when Carleton was present.

===Chinese===
而汤姆自己呢，早已一往情深地爱着马德琳，且已爱了多年。他一再恳求她嫁给他；她心底也明白，他的恳求出自他对她本人的爱，而丝毫不是看中她的财产。
然而，她一旦嫁了别人，叔父那笔财产的一切指望，对汤姆·威拉德来说便永远失去了。
不过，尽管汤姆挥霍成性、一掷千金，即便眼看家财将从指缝间溜走，他也毫无惋惜之色。除了动辄一点就着的突发火气，他总是快快活活、笑声感染众人，俨然已是眼下聚在马德琳屋檐下的这群宾客里的灵魂人物。
汤姆住进了范·诺曼宅——卡尔顿当然不便住进来——这使威拉德在这位准新郎面前占了先手，而他对此也绝非没有自觉。一半是为了撩拨那位不动声色却暗怀妒意的卡尔顿，一半是出于对表妹的真心喜爱，汤姆殷勤备至地陪伴马德琳，尤其当卡尔顿在场的时候。

===Original===
“You see, Maddy,” Tom would say, “there are only a few days left of our boy and girl chumminess. I fancy that after you are married, Schuyler won’t let me speak to you, save in most formal terms, so I must see all I can of you now.”
Then he would tuck her arm through his own, and take her for a stroll in the grounds, and Carleton, coming to search for her, would find them cosily chatting in a secluded arbor, or drifting lazily in a canoe on the tiny, lily-padded lake.
These things greatly annoyed Schuyler Carleton, but remonstrance was never an easy task for him, nor did it ever affect Madeleine pleasantly.

===Chinese===
“你瞧，玛迪，”汤姆总这么说，“咱们两小无猜的交情只剩几天了。我琢磨着等你结了婚，斯凯勒就不许我跟你说话了，除非拿最客套的言辞寒暄，所以趁现在，我能多见你几面就多见几面。”
说罢他便把她的小臂挽进自己臂弯，带她在庭园里散步；卡尔顿来找她时，总会发现他们俩正躲在幽僻的凉亭里惬意闲谈，或是乘着独木舟，在浮满睡莲的小湖上懒洋洋地漂荡。
这些事大大惹恼了斯凯勒·卡尔顿，可是劝谏于他从来不是件容易事，而马德琳听了也从不痛快。

===Original===
“I wish, Madeleine,” he had said one day, when he had waited two hours for her to return from a drive with Tom, “that you would have a little regard for appearances, if you have none for my wishes. It is not seemly for my betrothed wife to be driving all over the country with another man.”
Magnificent Madeleine looked straight at him, tilting her head back slightly to look beneath her half-closed lids.
“It is not seemly,” she said, “for my betrothed husband to imply that I could be at fault in a matter of propriety or punctilio. That is not possible.”
“You are right,” he said, and his eyes gleamed with admiration of her glorious beauty and imperious manner. “Forgive me﻿—you are indeed right.”
Though Schuyler Carleton may not have been lavish of affection, he begrudged no admiration to the splendid woman he had won.

===Chinese===
“马德琳，我希望，”有一天他说——那天他干等了两个钟头，等她同汤姆驾车兜风回来——“你即便全然不顾我的心愿，多少也顾一顾体面。我的未婚妻同另一个男人驾着车满乡野跑，这成何体统。”
雍容华贵的马德琳直直地望着他，头微微向后一仰，从半阖的眼帘下睨视着他。
“我的未婚夫暗示我可能在规矩礼数上有失检点，”她说，“这同样不成体统。那是绝不可能的事。”
“你说得对，”他说，眼中闪烁着对她那夺人的美貌与倨傲风姿的激赏，“原谅我——你确实是对的。”
斯凯勒·卡尔顿在感情上或许吝于表达，但对他赢得的这位光彩照人的女子，他的倾慕却毫不吝惜。

===Original===
And yet, had he but known it, the apparently scornful and haughty girl was craving a more tender and gentle love, and would gladly have foregone his admiration to have received more affection.
“But it will come,” Madeleine thought to herself. “I am not of the ‘clinging vine’ type, I know; but after we are married, surely Schuyler will be less formally polite, and more﻿—well﻿—chummy.”
Yet Madeleine herself was chummy with nobody save Tom.
They two were always chatting and laughing together, and though they differed sometimes, and even quarrelled, it was quickly made up, and forgotten in a new subject of merry discussion.
But, after all, they rarely quarrelled except regarding Madeleine’s approaching marriage.

===Chinese===
然而，倘使他能够知晓，这个外表冷傲的姑娘其实正渴盼着一份更温存、更柔情的爱；只要能得到更多的情意，她甘愿舍掉他的倾慕。
“不过，以后总会有的，”马德琳暗自想道，“我知道自己不是那种‘依人藤’般的性子；可是等我们结了婚，斯凯勒总该会少些客套，多些——呃——亲热随便吧。”
然而，马德琳自己除了汤姆，同谁都亲热不起来。
他们俩总是在一起又说又笑；虽则有时意见相左，甚至拌起嘴来，也转眼言归于好，抛在脑后，又兴致勃勃地谈起新的乐子。
不过说到底，他们很少拌嘴——除了为马德琳的婚事。

===Original===
“Don’t throw yourself away on that iceberg, Maddy,” Tom would plead. “He’s a truly fine man, I know, but he can’t make you happy.”
“How absurd you are, Tom! Give me credit, please, for knowing my own mind, at least. I love Schuyler Carleton, and I am proud that he is to be my husband. He is the finest man I have ever known in every way, and I am a fortunate girl to be chosen by such a man.”
“Oho, Maddy! Don’t do the humble; it doesn’t suit you at all. You are the type who ought to have ‘kings and crown princes at your feet.’ And Carleton is princely enough in his effects, but he’s by no means at your feet.”
“What do you mean?” exclaimed Madeleine angrily.
“Just what I say. Schuyler Carleton admires you greatly, but he doesn’t love you﻿—at least, not as I do!”

===Chinese===
“别把自己糟蹋在那座冰山上了，玛迪，”汤姆总这么劝她，“我知道他是个真正的好男人，可他没法让你幸福。”
“你多荒唐啊，汤姆！至少请相信，我自己的心意我还是明白的。我爱斯凯勒·卡尔顿，我为将做我丈夫的人是他而骄傲。他是我平生所识的男人中样样都最出色的一个，能被这样的人选中，我这姑娘算是好福气。”
“嗬，玛迪！别来这套低声下气，这可半点不合你的性子。你这种人，理该让‘国王和太子们都拜倒在你脚下’。论气派，卡尔顿倒真够王子的份，可他压根儿没有拜倒在你脚下。”
“你这话什么意思？”马德琳怒喝道。
“就是我说的意思。斯凯勒·卡尔顿非常倾慕你，可他并不爱你——至少，不像我这样爱你！”

===Original===
“Don’t be foolish, Tom. Naturally you know nothing about Mr. Carleton’s affection for me﻿—he does not proclaim it from the housetops. And I desire you not to speak of it again.”
“Why should I speak of what doesn’t exist? Forgive me, Maddy, but I love you so myself, it drives me frantic to see that man treating you so coolly.”
“He doesn’t treat me coolly. Or, if he does, it’s because I don’t wish for tender demonstrations before other people. I’m fond of you, Tom, as you know, but I won’t allow even you to criticise the man I am about to marry.”
“Oh, very well, marry him, then, and a precious unhappy life you’ll lead with him﻿—and I know why.”

===Chinese===
“别犯傻了，汤姆。你自然无从知晓卡尔顿先生对我的感情——他又不会站在屋顶上四处张扬。我不许你再提这件事。”
“不存在的东西，我提它做什么？原谅我，玛迪，只是我自己太爱你了，眼见那男人待你那么冷淡，我简直要发狂。”
“他待我并不冷淡。就算冷淡，那也是因为我不愿在外人面前有温存的表示。汤姆，你知道我待你有情，可哪怕是你，也不许非议我即将嫁的人。”
“哦，很好，那你就嫁给他吧，有你跟他过的苦日子——而且其中的缘故，我心里有数。”

===Original===
Madeleine turned on him, her eyes blazing with anger.
“What do you mean? Explain that last remark of yours.”
“Small need! You know why as well as I do;” and Tom pushed his hands into his pockets and strode away, whistling, well knowing that he had roused his cousin’s even temper at last.
In addition to some of her Mapleton friends, Madeleine had invited two girls from New York to be her bridesmaids. Kitty French and Molly Gardner had already come and were staying at the Van Norman house the few days that would intervene before the wedding.

===Chinese===
马德琳猛地转过身来对着他，双眼怒火灼灼。
“你什么意思？把你最后那句话给我说清楚。”
“何必多说！缘由你我心里都明白。”汤姆把双手往口袋里一插，吹着口哨大步走开，心里清楚，自己这回总算把表妹那副一贯沉静的脾气给惹动了。
除了几位梅普尔顿的闺中女友，马德琳还从纽约（New York）请来两位姑娘做伴娘。基蒂·弗伦奇（Kitty French）与莫莉·加德纳（Molly Gardner）已经到了，正住在范·诺曼宅里，等着婚礼前的这几天过去。

===Original===
Knowing Madeleine well, as they did, they had not expected confidence from her, nor did they look forward to cosy, romantic boudoir chats, such as many girls would enjoy.
But neither had they expected the peculiar constraint that seemed to hang over all the members of the household.
Mrs. Markham had been so long housekeeper, and even companion, for Madeleine that she was not looked upon as a servant, and to her Kitty French put a few discreet questions regarding the exceeding reserve of Mr. Carleton.

===Chinese===
她们深知马德琳的为人，既不曾指望她吐露心曲，也不曾指望能有那种许多姑娘都喜欢的、闺房里温馨旖旎的私房话。
可她们同样没有料到，这宅子里的上上下下，竟仿佛都笼罩着一层说不出的拘谨。
马卡姆太太为马德琳管家多年，甚至兼做她的女伴，早已不被当下人看待。基蒂·弗伦奇便向她婉转地问起几句，打听卡尔顿先生何以那般过分矜持。

===Original===
“I don’t know, Miss French,” said the good woman, looking sadly disturbed. “I love Madeleine as I would my own child. I know she adores Mr. Carleton﻿—and﻿—yes, I know he greatly admires her﻿—and yet there *is* something wrong. I can’t express it﻿—it’s merely a feeling﻿—an intuition, but there *is* something wrong.”
“You know Mr. Willard is in love with Maddy,” suggested Miss French.
“Oh, it isn’t that. They’ve always had a cousinly affection for each other, and﻿—yes, Tom is in love with her﻿—but what I mean is aside from all that. The real reason that Madeleine flirts with Tom﻿—for she *does* flirt with him﻿—is to pique Mr. Carleton. There! I’ve said more than I meant to, but you’re too good a friend to let it make any trouble, and, anyway, in a few days they will be married, and then I’m sure it will be all right﻿—I’m *sure* of it.”
Like many people, Mrs. Markham emphasized by repetition a statement of whose truth she was far from sure.

===Chinese===
“我说不上来，弗伦奇小姐，”这位好心肠的妇人说道，神色戚然，“我爱马德琳，就像爱我自己的孩子。我知道她深爱着卡尔顿先生——而且——是的，我知道他也十分倾慕她——可是，事情确实有些不对劲。我说不清楚——那只是一种感觉——一种直觉，可事情确实有些不对。”
“您知道威拉德先生爱着玛迪吧。”弗伦奇小姐提了一句。
“哦，不是那个。他们俩向来是表亲间的情谊，而且——是的，汤姆是爱着她——可我指的不是这些。马德琳之所以同汤姆打情骂俏——她确实在同他打情骂俏——真正的缘由，是要激一激卡尔顿先生。瞧，我说得比本意多了；不过你是个再好不过的朋友，不会让这话惹出什么是非；再说，反正不出几天他们就要成婚了，到那时我担保一切都会好起来——我担保。”
同许多人一样，马卡姆太太把一句自己远谈不上有把握的话，翻来覆去地说着，仿佛多说几遍便能成真。

## II. Miss Morton Arrives / 二　莫顿小姐到来

===Original===
The day before the wedding the old house was a pleasant scene of bustle and confusion.

Professional decorators were in charge of the great drawing-room, building a canopy of green vines and flowers, beneath which the bridal pair should stand the next day at high noon.

This work was greatly hindered by a bevy of young people who thought they were helping.

At last, noting a look of dumb exasperation on the face of one of the florist’s men, Molly Gardner exclaimed, “I don’t believe our help is needed here; come on, Kitty, let’s go in the library and wait for teatime.”

It was nearly five o’clock, and the girls found most of the house guests already assembled in the library, awaiting the arrival of the tea-tray.

Several other young people were there also, most of them being those who were to be of the wedding cortège next day.

===Chinese===
婚礼前一天，老宅里呈现出一派热闹欢腾、人来人往的宜人景象。

大客厅由专业装饰师主持布置，他们正用青藤和鲜花搭起一座华盖，次日正午时分，新婚伉俪便将立于其下。

这番工程却被一群自以为在帮忙的年轻人大大耽误了。

终于，莫莉·加德纳瞧见花匠伙计脸上那副敢怒不敢言的神气，便嚷道：“我看我们在这儿帮不上什么忙了；来吧，基蒂，咱们上书房去，等着吃茶点。”

将近五点钟，两位姑娘来到书房，发现宅中留宿的宾客大多已经聚在那里，只等茶盘送上来。

另有几位年轻人也在场，其中大半是次日要列于婚礼仪仗之中的人。

===Original===
Robert Fessenden, who was to be best man, had just come from New York, and had dropped in to see Miss Van Norman.

Although he was an old friend of Carleton’s, Madeleine did not know him very well, and though she made him welcome, it was with that coldly formal air that did not greatly attract the young man, but he could not fail to be impressed by her great beauty.

“Lucky fellow, Carleton,” he said to Tom Willard. “Why, that woman would create a sensation in any great city in the world.”

“Yes, she is too handsome to live all her life in a small village,” agreed Tom. “I think they intend to travel a great deal.”

“An heiress, too, I believe.”

“Yes, she has all the desirable traits a woman can possess.”

===Chinese===
将在婚礼上担任伴郎的罗伯特·费森登（Robert Fessenden）刚从纽约来到，顺道进宅拜望范·诺曼小姐。

他虽是卡尔顿的老朋友，马德琳与他却并不熟稔；她虽也以礼相迎，那股冷冰冰的客套气却不大引得起这位年轻人的好感——只是她那惊人的美貌，任谁也无法视而不见。

“卡尔顿真是个幸运的家伙，”他对汤姆·威拉德说，“嗬，这样的女子，无论到世界上哪一座大都会，都要轰动一时。”

“可不是，她这样的品貌，一辈子窝在小小的村子里，未免可惜，”汤姆随声附和，“我想他们往后是要大大地游历一番的。”

“我想，还是位女继承人呢。”

“是啊，女人家所能具备的种种可心品质，她可是一样不缺。”

===Original===
“All?” Fessenden’s tone was quizzical.

“What do you mean?” asked Tom sharply.

“Nothing; only, if I were to marry, I should prefer a little more softness of nature.”

“Oh, that’s only her manner. My cousin is most sweet and womanly, I assure you.”

“I’m sure she is,” returned Fessenden, who was a bit ashamed of his outspokenness; “and she’s getting a sterling good fellow for a husband.”

“She is so,” said Tom, heartily, which was kind of him, considering his own opinion of Carleton.

===Chinese===
“一样不缺？”费森登的语气带着揶揄。

“你这话是什么意思？”汤姆沉下脸问。

“没什么；只不过，若换作是我娶亲，我倒情愿女方的性子和软温存一些。”

“嗐，那不过是她的做派。我这表妹呀，性子最温柔娇婉不过，我向你担保。”

“那是自然，”费森登答道，为自己方才的直言快语有点不好意思，“她要嫁的，也的确是位成色十足的好人。”

“可不是嘛。”汤姆诚心诚意地说——想想他自己对卡尔顿的那点看法，这话说得实在算厚道了。

===Original===
And then both men strolled over to where Madeleine sat at the tea-table. She was reading a telegram that had just been brought to her, and she laughingly explained to Tom that it meant a bother for him.

“Miss Morton has concluded to come to the wedding, after all,” she said. “She wrote me that she wouldn’t come, but she has changed her mind, it seems. Now, it does sound ridiculous, I know, but in this big house there isn’t a room left for her but the one you have, Tom. You see, one bedroom is used for a ‘present room,’ one is reserved for Schuyler tomorrow, the bridesmaids have another, and except for our own rooms, and those already occupied by guests, there are no more. I hate to ask you, Tom, but could you go to the Inn?”

“Sure, Maddy dear; anything to oblige. But it does seem too bad to turn me out of your house the very last day that your hospitality is all your own to offer. Tomorrow the grand Seigneur will be master here, and my timid little Madeleine can no longer call her soul her own.”

This reference to the tall and stately mistress of the house raised a general laugh, but Madeleine did not join in it.

“I’m so sorry, Tom,” she said earnestly, as she looked again at the telegram she was holding, “but Miss Morton was an old friend of Uncle Richard’s, and as she wants to come here I can’t turn her away. And unless you give her your room, there is no other﻿—”

“Nonsense, Madeleine! I’m only joking. Of course I’ll go to the hotel. Only too glad to accommodate Miss Morton. Forget it, girl; I assure you I don’t mind a bit. I’ll pack up a few traps after dinner and skip down to the picturesque, if rather ostentatious, Mapleton Inn.”

===Chinese===
随后两人便踱到茶几旁马德琳的座处。她正读着一封刚送到的电报，笑着向汤姆解释说，这封信要给他添点小小的麻烦。

“莫顿小姐（Miss Morton）到底还是决定来参加婚礼了，”她说，“她先前写信告诉我说不来了，看样子又变了主意。我知道，这要求听着实在荒唐，可这么大一所房子，除了你住的那间，再没有多余的房间给她了。你想呀，一间卧室做了‘礼品间’，一间明天要留给斯凯勒，伴娘们又占了一间，除了我们自家的屋子，再加上宾客们已经住下的，就再没有了。我真不愿开这个口，汤姆，可你能不能搬去旅馆住？”

“没问题，亲爱的玛迪；乐意效劳。只可惜啊，你自主张罗待客的最后一天，偏要把我扫地出门。明天那位气派十足的大领主就要成为这里的主人了，我那怯生生的小马德琳，可就再不能自己说了算喽。”

这话打趣的正是那位身材高挑、仪态威严的宅邸女主人，引得众人一阵哄笑，马德琳自己却没有跟着笑。

“真对不起，汤姆，”她又看了看手里的电报，恳切地说，“可莫顿小姐是理查德叔父的老朋友，她既然想要来，我总不能把她拒之门外。可要是你不把房间让给她，就实在没有别的——”

“哪儿的话，马德琳！我不过是说笑罢了。我当然搬去旅馆住。能给莫顿小姐行个方便，再乐意不过。快别放在心上，姑娘；我担保自己一点也不介意。晚饭后我收拾几件行李，就溜达到那座景色宜人——只是稍嫌铺张——的梅普尔顿旅馆（Mapleton Inn）去。”

===Original===
As Tom spoke he put his arm carelessly round Madeleine’s shoulders, and though scarcely more than a cousinly caress, it was unfortunate that Schuyler Carleton should enter the room at that moment. A lightning glance flashed between the two men, and as Tom moved away from Madeleine with a slightly embarrassed shrug of his shoulders, Carleton’s face grew so stern that an uncomfortable silence fell upon the guests.

However, the arrival of the tea-tray saved the situation, and Madeleine at once busied herself in the pretty occupation of serving tea to her guests.

With an air of jealous proprietorship, Carleton moved toward her and, looking handsome, though sulky, stood by Willard with folded arms, as if on guard.

Urged on by a daredevil spirit of mischief, and perhaps remembering that Madeleine would soon be beyond his reach as Carleton’s wife, Tom also moved toward her from the other side. Endeavoring to treat the situation lightly, Madeleine held up a newly-filled teacup.

“Who will have this?” she asked gaily.

“I will!” declared Carleton and Tom at the same time, and each held out a hand.

===Chinese===
汤姆说这话时，随手把一条胳膊搭上了马德琳的肩头。这举动虽说不过是表亲之间的亲昵，不巧的是，斯凯勒·卡尔顿偏偏在此时走进了房间。两个男人之间闪过一道电光石火般的对视，汤姆微微耸了耸肩，略带尴尬地从马德琳身边走开；卡尔顿的脸色却沉得吓人，一时间，满座宾客都不自在地沉默了下来。

幸而茶盘恰在此时端了进来，替众人解了围，马德琳立刻忙起那桩赏心悦目的差事——为宾客们斟茶。

卡尔顿带着一股醋意十足的主人架势朝她走去；他在威拉德身旁站定，双臂抱胸，一派如临大敌的守卫模样，那张脸英俊归英俊，却始终绷着愠色。

汤姆被一股胆大妄为的顽劣劲儿鼓动着——或许也惦记着马德琳不久便要成为卡尔顿的妻子，从此再非他所能亲近——也从另一侧朝她凑了过去。马德琳竭力想把这一幕轻轻带过，便举起一只刚斟满的茶杯。

“这一杯给谁呀？”她快活地问。

“给我！”卡尔顿与汤姆异口同声，各自伸出手来。

===Original===
Madeleine looked at them both smilingly.

Carleton’s face was white and set; he was evidently making a serious matter of the trifling episode.

Tom, on the contrary, was smiling broadly, and was quite evidently enjoying his rival’s discomfiture.

“I shall give it to you, because you look so pleasant,” declared Madeleine, handing the cup to Tom. “Now, Schuyler, smile prettily and you may have one, too.”

But Carleton would not fall in with her light mood.

Bending a little, he said in a tense voice, “I will leave you to your cousin now. Tomorrow I shall assert my claim.”

===Chinese===
马德琳笑吟吟地望望这个，又望望那个。

卡尔顿面色发白，神情僵硬；他分明是要把这段区区小插曲当作正经大事来计较了。

汤姆却正相反，笑得合不拢嘴，明摆着正在欣赏情敌的狼狈。

“这杯给你，谁教你模样儿这么讨人喜欢。”马德琳说着，把茶杯递到汤姆手里，“好了，斯凯勒，也甜甜地笑一个，你就照样有一杯。”

卡尔顿却偏不肯顺着她这份轻快劲儿。

他微微俯下身去，声音紧绷地说：“现在，我把你交还给你的表哥。到了明天，我就要主张我的权利了。”

===Original===
Though not rude in themselves, the words were accompanied by a harsh and disdainful glance that made several of the onlookers wonder what sort of a life the haughty Madeleine would lead with such a coldly tyrannical husband.

“The brute!” said Tom, under his breath, as Carleton left the room. “Never mind, Maddy, the old Turk has left you to me for this evening, and we’ll take him at his word.”

Suddenly Madeleine’s mood changed to one of utter gaiety. She smiled impartially on all, she jested with the girls, she bewitched the young men with her merry banter, and she almost seemed to be flirting with Tom Willard. But he was her cousin, after all, and much is forgiven a bride-to-be on her wedding eve.

Robert Fessenden looked at Miss Van Norman with a puzzled air. He couldn’t seem to understand her, and was glad when by chance the two were left comparatively alone for a few moments’ conversation.

“A great responsibility devolves on the best man, Miss Van Norman,” he said, in response to a chaffing remark of hers. “I suppose that tomorrow I shall be general director-in-chief, and if anything *should* go wrong, I shall be blamed.”

“But nothing *will* go wrong,” said Madeleine, gaily, “and then, think how you’ll be praised!”

===Chinese===
这话本身虽算不得无礼，出口时却伴着一道冷厉轻蔑的眼色，令旁观者中好几位暗自纳罕：高傲的马德琳跟着这样一位冷面专制的丈夫，往后的日子不知要过成什么样子。

“好个蛮子！”卡尔顿走出房间后，汤姆低声骂道，“别理他，玛迪。这位土耳其老爷今晚总算把你留给了我，咱们就承他的情，照他的话办。”

马德琳的情绪忽然一变，变得说不出的欢快。她对众人一视同仁地展露笑靥，同姑娘们打趣，用俏皮的妙语把年轻小伙子们迷得团团转，对汤姆·威拉德简直像是在卖弄风情。不过他到底是她的表哥，况且待嫁的新娘在婚礼前夜，做什么都容易得到原谅。

罗伯特·费森登一脸困惑地望着范·诺曼小姐。他似乎怎么也捉摸不透这个人，因此当两人碰巧相对单独地待上片刻、可以说几句体己话时，他不禁暗自高兴。

“伴郎肩上的责任可重大呢，范·诺曼小姐，”他是应着她一句打趣话而说的，“照我看，明天我就该是总指挥了，万一当真出了什么岔子，挨骂的自然是我。”

“可什么岔子也不会出呀，”马德琳欢快地说，“到那时你再想想，人家该怎样夸你呢！”

===Original===
“Ah, but you won’t be here to hear the praise heaped upon me, so what’s the use?”

“No, I shall be gone forever,” said Madeleine, putting on one of her faraway looks. “I never want to come back to Mapleton. I hate it!”

“Why, Miss Van Norman! You want to desert this beautiful old house? Schuyler can never find you a home so comfortable and attractive in every way.”

“I don’t care. I want to go far away from Mapleton to live. We’re going to travel for a year, anyway, but when we do settle down, it will be abroad, I hope.”

“You surprise me. Schuyler didn’t tell me this. We’ve been chums so long, that I usually know of his plans. But, of course, getting married changes all that.”

“You’re a very intimate friend of Mr. Carleton’s, aren’t you?” said Madeleine, with a strange note of wistfulness in her voice.

===Chinese===
“唉，可到那时你已不在这里，听不见人家把夸赞往我身上堆，那夸了还有什么用处？”

“是啊，我要一去不回了。”马德琳说着，面上浮起她那种恍惚悠远的神情，“我再不想回梅普尔顿来了。我恨这个地方！”

“哎呀，范·诺曼小姐！您要舍弃这座美丽的老宅吗？斯凯勒到哪里再给您找一处这样舒适、这样处处可心的家呢？”

“我才不管呢。我要住到离梅普尔顿远远的地方去。反正我们打算去旅行一年；等真要安顿下来的时候，我希望是在国外。”

“这可真叫我吃惊。斯凯勒从没对我说起过。我们俩交情这么深，他的打算我向来一清二楚。不过，成了亲，这些自然就另当别论了。”

“您是卡尔顿先生极亲近的朋友，是么？”马德琳说，声音里带着一缕奇异的怅惘。

===Original===
“Yes, I am. Why?”

“Oh, nothing; I only thought﻿—I mean, do you think﻿—”

Rob Fessenden was thrilled by the plaintive expression on the beautiful face, and suddenly felt a great desire to help this girl, who was seemingly so far above and beyond all need of help, and yet was surely about to ask his aid, or at least his sympathy.

“Don’t hesitate,” he said gently; “what is it, Miss Van Norman? I want to be as firm a friend of yours as I am of Schuyler’s, so please say what you wish to.”

“I can’t﻿—I can’t,” Madeleine whispered, and her voice was almost a moan.

“Please,” again urged Fessenden.

===Chinese===
“是的，很要好。怎么？”

“哦，没什么；我只是想——我是说，您认为——”

罗布·费森登被那张美丽面孔上哀婉的神情打动了，心里陡然涌起一股强烈的愿望，想要帮助这位姑娘——她看上去那样高不可攀，仿佛根本无需任何人援手，此刻却分明是有求于他，至少也是渴求他的一份同情。

“别犹豫，”他温言道，“是什么事，范·诺曼小姐？我愿意做您坚实可靠的朋友，就像我做斯凯勒的朋友那样，所以，有什么想说的请尽管说吧。”

“我说不出口——说不出口啊。”马德琳喃喃低语，嗓音几乎成了一声呜咽。

“请说吧。”费森登再次催促道。

===Original===
“Do you know Dorothy Burt?” Madeleine then broke out, as if the words were fairly forced from her.

“No,” said Fessenden, amazed; “I never heard the name before. Who is she?”

“Hush! She’s nobody﻿—less than nobody. Don’t mention her to me ever again﻿—nor to anyone else. Ah, here comes Miss Morton.”

As Fessenden watched Madeleine, she changed swiftly from a perturbed, troubled girl to a courteous, polished hostess.

“My dear Miss Morton,” she said, advancing to meet her newest guest, “how kind of you to come to me at this time.”

“I didn’t come exactly out of kindness,” said Miss Morton, “but because I desired to come. I hope you are quite well. Will you give me some tea?”

===Chinese===
“您认识多萝西·伯特（Dorothy Burt）吗？”马德琳终于脱口而出，仿佛这话是被硬生生从她嘴里挤出来的。

“不认识，”费森登愕然道，“这个名字我从来没有听见过。她是谁？”

“嘘！她什么也不是——比什么都不是还不如。往后别再向我提起她——对任何人也别提。啊，莫顿小姐来了。”

费森登眼看着马德琳霎时间换了一个人：方才还是心绪烦乱的姑娘，转眼已是谦和有礼、圆熟周到的女主人。

“亲爱的莫顿小姐，”她迎上前去接待这位新到的客人，“您肯在这个时候赶来陪我，真是太难得了。”

“我可不全是为了好意才来的，”莫顿小姐说，“是因为我自己想来。希望你一切都好。请给我一杯茶，好吗？”

===Original===
Miss Morton was a tall, angular lady, with gray hair and sharp, black eyes. She seemed to bite off her words at the ends of her short sentences, and had a brisk, alert manner that was, in a way, aggressive.

“An eccentric,” Rob Fessenden thought, as he looked at her, and wondered why she was there at all.

“An old sweetheart of Mr. Richard Van Norman, I believe,” said Kitty French, when he questioned her. “They were once engaged and then quarrelled and broke it off, and neither of them lived happily ever after.”

“As the Carletons *will*,” said Fessenden, smiling.

“Yes,” said Kitty slowly, “as the Carletons will﻿—I hope. You know Mr. Carleton awfully well, don’t you? Are you sure he will make our Maddy happy, Mr. Fessenden?”

“I think so;” and Fessenden tried to speak casually. “He is not an emotional man, or one greatly given to sentiment, but I judge she is not that sort either.”

===Chinese===
莫顿小姐是位又高又瘦、棱角分明的女士，头发灰白，一双锐利的黑眼睛。她说话句子短促，仿佛字字到了句尾都要被咬断似的；那副干脆利落、机警逼人的做派，多少带着几分咄咄之气。

“好个古怪人。”罗布·费森登打量着她，心里暗想，一面纳闷她究竟为什么会到这儿来。

“我想，她是理查德·范·诺曼先生昔日的恋人，”他向基蒂·弗伦奇打听时，她这样答道，“他们俩曾经订过婚，后来吵翻了，婚约作罢——从此啊，谁也没能‘幸福地生活下去’。”

“卡尔顿家嘛，偏要‘从此幸福地生活下去’。”费森登笑着应道。

“是啊，”基蒂慢悠悠地说，“偏要幸福地生活下去——但愿如此。您同卡尔顿先生熟得很，是不是？您能拿准他会让我们的玛迪幸福吗，费森登先生？”

“我看会的。”费森登尽量把话说得随意，“他不是个感情外露的人，也谈不上多愁善感，不过我料想，她也不是那一类的性子。”

===Original===
“Oh, yes, she is! Maddy is apparently cold and cynical, but she isn’t really so a bit. But she perfectly adores him, and if they’re not happy, it won’t be her fault.”

“Nor will it be his,” said Fessenden, warmly defending his absent friend. “Carleton’s an old trump. There’s no finer man in the world, and any woman ought to be happy with him.”

“I’m glad to hear you say that,” said Kitty, with a little sigh of relief. “Do look at that funny Miss Morton! She seems to be scolding Madeleine. I’m sorry she came. She doesn’t seem very attractive. But perhaps it’s because she was crossed in love and it made her queer.”

“Or she was queered in love and it made her cross,” laughed Fessenden. “Well, I must go, now, and look up Carleton. Poor old boy, he was a little miffed when he went away.”

After tea all the callers departed, and those who were house guests went to their rooms to dress for dinner.

===Chinese===
“嗳，她恰恰是！玛迪表面上冷淡又爱讥诮，骨子里却一点也不是那样。可她把他崇拜得五体投地，他们俩要是不幸福，决计怪不到她头上。”

“也怪不到他头上。”费森登热切地回护着不在场的朋友，“卡尔顿是条百里挑一的好汉子。天底下再没有比他更好的人了，哪个女人跟了他都该幸福才是。”

“听您这么说我就放心了。”基蒂说着，轻轻舒了一口气，“快瞧那位古怪的莫顿小姐！她像是在数落马德琳呢。真遗憾她来了。她瞧着可不大讨人喜欢。不过，兴许是她在恋爱上受过挫折，人才变得这么古怪吧。”

“要不就是她这个人先就古里古怪，才把恋情弄砸，憋出一肚子火。”费森登哈哈一笑，“好了，这会儿我得走了，去看看卡尔顿。可怜的老伙计，他走的时候多少有点悻悻然呢。”

茶点过后，来客尽数散去，留宿宅中的宾客则各自回房，更衣准备晚宴。

===Original===
Tom Willard, with great show of burlesque regret and tearful farewells, went to the hotel, that Miss Morton might have the room he had been occupying.

He promised to return for dinner, and gaily blew kisses to Madeleine as with his traps he was driven down the avenue.

At dinner, Schuyler Carleton’s place was vacant. It had been arranged next to Madeleine’s, and when fifteen minutes after the dinner hour he had not arrived, she haughtily accepted Tom Willard’s arm and led the way to the dining-room.

But having reached the table, she directed Tom to take his rightful seat, at some distance from her own, and Carleton’s chair remained empty at Madeleine’s side.

At first this was uncomfortably evident, but Madeleine was in gay spirits, and soon the whole party followed her lead, and the conversation was general and in a merry key.

The young hostess had never looked more regally beautiful. Her dark hair, piled high on her head, was adorned with a dainty ornament which, though only a twisted ribbon, was shaped like a crown, and gave her the effect of an imperious queen. Her low-cut gown of pale yellow satin was severe of line and accented her stately bearing, while her exquisitely modelled neck and shoulders were as white and pure as those of a marble statue. Save for a double row of pearls around her throat, she wore no ornaments, but on the morrow Carleton’s gift of magnificent diamonds would grace her bridal costume. The combination of haughty imperial beauty and a dazzling witchery of mood was irresistible, and the men and girls alike realized that never before had Madeleine seemed so wonderful.

===Chinese===
汤姆·威拉德做张做智，把滑稽的惋惜与泪汪汪的告辞演了个十足，动身去了旅馆，好让他原先住的那间屋子腾给莫顿小姐。

他答应回来吃晚饭；后来车子载着他和小件行李沿林荫道驶下去时，他还快活地朝马德琳频送飞吻。

晚宴时，斯凯勒·卡尔顿的座位空着。那座位原是安排在马德琳身旁的；开宴时刻过了十五分钟，他仍不见人影，马德琳便傲然挽起汤姆·威拉德的手臂，带头走向餐厅。

但到了桌边，她却吩咐汤姆坐到他自己应坐的位子上去——那座位离她颇有一段距离——于是卡尔顿的椅子依旧空悬在她身旁。

起初，这个空位子瞧着让人怪不自在，无奈马德琳兴致高昂，众人不久便都跟着她的引领谈笑起来，席间言谈无拘无束，一派欢快。

年轻的女主人从未显得这般雍容华贵、光彩照人。她一头乌发高高挽起，上面簪着一件精巧的饰物——虽只是一段挽结的缎带，却恰似王冠的样式，使她俨然一位威仪凛凛的王后。她那袭浅黄缎子的低领礼服线条素净，愈发衬出她端庄挺拔的气度；那浑然天成的颈项与双肩，白皙莹洁，直如大理石雕像一般。除了颈间一串双圈的珍珠，她通身不戴别的首饰；而到了明天，卡尔顿所赠的那套璀璨夺目的钻石，将为她的新娘装束更增华彩。高傲尊贵的绝色，与摄人心魄的妩媚情致交融在一处，教人无从抗拒——无论男子还是姑娘们都感觉到，马德琳从未像今晚这样明艳动人。

===Original===
After the dessert was placed on the table, Willard could stand it no longer, and, leaving his own place, he calmly appropriated Carleton’s vacant chair.

Madeleine did not reprove him, and Kitty French took occasion to whisper to her neighbor:

“ ‘ ’Twere better by far to have matched our fair cousin to brave Lochinvar.’ ”

Mrs. Markham overheard the quotation, and a look of pain came into her eyes. But it was all too late now, and tomorrow Madeleine would be irrevocably Schuyler Carleton’s wife.

After dinner coffee was served in the cozy library. Madeleine preferred this room to the more elaborately furnished drawing-room, and tonight her word was law.

But suddenly her mood changed. For no apparent reason her gay spirits vanished, her smile faded away, and a pathetic droop curved the corners of her beautiful mouth.

===Chinese===
甜点上桌之后，威拉德再也按捺不住，索性离了自己的座位，若无其事地坐上了卡尔顿那把空椅子。

马德琳并没有苛责他，基蒂·弗伦奇便乘机凑近邻座，低声念道：

“‘把咱们美丽的表妹许给英勇的洛钦瓦尔（Lochinvar），原该好得多。’”

这句引文给马卡姆太太听了个正着，她眼中掠过一丝痛楚。可如今说什么都太迟了，明天一到，马德琳便无可挽回地成为斯凯勒·卡尔顿的妻子。

晚宴之后，咖啡奉在温馨舒适的书房里。比起陈设更为讲究的大客厅，马德琳更偏爱这间屋子，而今晚，她的话便是律令。

可她的心情陡然又变了。无缘无故地，满腔欢快烟消云散，笑意从脸上褪尽，那两片美丽的嘴角可怜巴巴地垂了下来。

===Original===
At about ten o’clock she said abruptly, though gently, “I wish you’d all go to bed. Unless you girls get some beauty sleep, you won’t look pretty at my wedding tomorrow.”

“I’m quite ready to go,” declared Kitty French with some tact, for she saw that Madeleine was nervous and strung up to a high tension.

“I, too,” exclaimed Molly Gardner, and the two girls said good night and went upstairs.

Two or three young men who had been dinner guests also made their adieux, and Tom Willard said, “Well, I may as well toddle to my comforts of home, as understood by a country innkeeper.”

Madeleine said good night to him kindly enough, but without jest or gaiety. Tom looked at her curiously for a moment, and then, gently kissing her hand, he went away.

Mrs. Markham, having seen Miss Morton comfortably installed in what had been Tom’s room, returned to the library to offer her services to Madeleine.

===Chinese===
十点钟光景，她忽然开口——话说得突兀，语气却温存：“我盼着你们都去睡吧。姑娘们要是不好好睡个美容觉，明天在我的婚礼上可就要不漂亮啦。”

“我这就去睡。”基蒂·弗伦奇乖觉地应道——她已看出马德琳心神不宁，整个人绷得像上了满弦。

“我也是。”莫莉·加德纳跟着应声。两位姑娘道过晚安，上楼去了。

两三位前来赴宴的年轻人也起身作别。汤姆·威拉德说：“得，我也该一步三摇，回去享受我那‘家一般的安乐窝’了——自然，是照乡下客栈老板所理解的那种。”

马德琳向他道晚安，态度足够和善，却没了往日的玩笑与欢声。汤姆诧异地望了她片刻，随后轻轻吻了吻她的手，走了。

马卡姆太太看着莫顿小姐在汤姆原先那间屋里舒舒服服地安顿下来，便回到书房，听候马德琳的差遣。

===Original===
But the girl only thanked her, saying, “There is nothing you can do tonight. I want to be alone for an hour or two. I will stay here in the library for a time, and I’d like to have you send Cicely to me.”

A few moments later Cicely Dupuy came in, bringing some letters and papers. She was Miss Van Norman’s private secretary, and admirably did she fill the post. Quick-witted, clever, deft of hand and brain, she answered notes, kept accounts, and in many ways made herself invaluable to her employer.

Moreover, Madeleine liked her. Cicely was of a charming personality. Small, fair, with big, childish blue eyes and a rose-leaf skin, she was a pretty picture to look at.

“Sit down,” said Madeleine, “and make a little list of some final matters I want you to attend to tomorrow.”

===Chinese===
姑娘只是谢了她，说：“今晚没有什么要您效劳的。我想独自待上一两个钟头。我就在书房里坐一会儿，劳您打发西塞莉到我这里来。”

片刻之后，西塞莉·杜皮伊（Cicely Dupuy）拿着几封信函和文件走了进来。她是范·诺曼小姐的私人秘书，而这桩差事她当得再出色不过。她心思灵敏，为人聪敏，手脑俱快：代复书简、掌管账目，样样拿得起，处处让主人离她不得。

况且马德琳也喜欢她。西塞莉生性可亲，个子娇小，眉眼浅淡，一双孩子气的大蓝眼睛，肌肤细嫩得如同玫瑰花瓣，整个人瞧上去便是一幅可爱的画儿。

“坐下吧，”马德琳说，“开一张小单子，把几件我要你明天办妥的最后事项记下来。”

===Original===
Cicely sat down, and, taking pencil and tablet from the library table, made the lists as Madeleine directed. This occupied but a short time, and then Miss Van Norman said wearily:

“You may go now, Cicely. Go to bed at once, dear. You will have much to do tomorrow. And please tell Marie I shall not need her services tonight. She may go to her room. I shall sit here for an hour or more, and I will answer these notes. I wish to be alone.”

“Very well, Miss Van Norman,” said Cicely, and, taking the lists she had made, she went softly from the room.

===Chinese===
西塞莉坐下来，从书房桌上取了铅笔和便笺簿，照马德琳的口述一一开列。这事没多久便办完了，随后范·诺曼小姐倦然说道：

“你可以去了，西塞莉。马上睡去吧，亲爱的。明天你有的是事要忙。还有，请告诉玛丽（Marie），今晚我用不着她伺候了，让她回自己房里去吧。我还要在这儿坐上一个钟头或者更久，把这些信都回复掉。我想独自待着。”

“好的，范·诺曼小姐。”西塞莉说。她拿起开好的单子，轻手轻脚地出了房门。

## III. A Cry in the Night / 三　暗夜惊呼

===Original===
“Help!”

The loud cry of a single word was not repeated, but repetition was unnecessary, for the sound rang through the old Van Norman house, and carried its message of fear and horror to all, awake or sleeping, within its walls.

It was about half-past eleven that same night, and Cicely Dupuy, still fully dressed, flew from her bedroom out into the hall.

Seeing a light downstairs, and hearing the servants’ bells, one after another, as if rung by a frantic hand, she hesitated a moment only, and then ran downstairs.

In the lower hall Schuyler Carleton, with a dazed expression on his white, drawn face, was uncertainly pushing various electric buttons which, in turn, flashed lights on or off, or rang bells in distant parts of the house.
===Chinese===
“救命啊！”

这一声高呼只喊了一个字，没有再喊第二遍，然而也无须再喊——那声音响彻了古老的范·诺曼宅邸，宅墙之内，无论醒着的还是睡着的，每一个人都听到了这满含恐惧与惊骇的消息。

还是当天夜里，约莫十一点半光景，西塞莉·杜皮伊衣裳尚未换下，从卧室飞奔而出，直冲进走廊。

她望见楼下亮着灯火，又听得仆人的铃铛一声接一声地响，仿佛被一只狂乱的手按个不住。她只迟疑了片刻，便奔下楼去。

楼下大厅里，斯凯勒·卡尔顿正迟疑无措地按着一个个电钮，惨白紧绷的脸上尽是茫然之色；那些电钮忽而把灯火点亮又熄灭，忽而叫响宅子远处的铃铛。

===Original===
For a moment Cicely stared straight at the man. Their eyes met, their gaze seemed to concentrate, and they stood motionless, as if spellbound.

This crisis was broken in upon by Marie, Madeleine’s French maid, who came running downstairs in a hastily donned negligee.

“*Mon Dieu!*” she cried. “*Ou est Mademoiselle?*”

With a start, Carleton turned from Cicely, and still with that dazed look on his face, he motioned Marie toward the wide doorway of the library. The girl took a step toward the threshold, and then, with a shriek, paused, and ventured no further.

Cicely, as if impelled by an unseen force, slowly turned and followed Marie’s movements, and as the girl screamed, Cicely grasped her tightly by the arm, and the two stood staring in at the library door.

What they saw was Madeleine Van Norman, seated in a chair at the library table. Her right arm was on the table, and her head, which had fallen to one side, was supported by her right shoulder. Her eyes were partly closed, and her lips were parted, and the position of the rigid figure left no need for further evidence that this was not a natural sleep.
===Chinese===
有那么一瞬，西塞莉直勾勾地瞪着这个男人。两人四目相接，目光仿佛凝在了一处，都一动不动地站着，如中了定身咒一般。

这一僵局被玛丽打破了——这位马德琳的法国贴身女仆披着一件匆匆套上的晨衣，一路奔下楼来。

“Mon Dieu!”她叫道，“Ou est Mademoiselle?”

卡尔顿猛然一惊，从西塞莉身上转过脸来，脸上仍是那副茫然的神情，朝书房宽敞的门口摆了摆手，示意玛丽过去。那姑娘朝门槛才跨出一步，便一声尖叫，顿住脚，再也不敢向前。

西塞莉仿佛受了一股无形力量的驱使，缓缓转过身去，目光追随着玛丽的动静；待那姑娘尖叫出声，西塞莉便一把紧紧攥住她的手臂，两人立在书房门口，朝屋里呆呆望去。

她们望见的，是坐在书房桌旁椅中的马德琳·范·诺曼。她右臂搁在桌上，头垂向一边，由右肩支着；双眼半阖，双唇微启。那僵直的身姿已足以表明，这绝非一场自然的睡眠，无须再有别的证据。

===Original===
But further evidence there was. Miss Van Norman still wore her yellow satin gown, but the beautiful embroidered bodice was stained a dull red, and a crimson stream was even then spreading its way down the shimmering breadths of the trailing skirt.

On the table, near the outstretched white hand, lay a Venetian dagger. This dagger was well known to the onlookers. It had lain on the library table for many years, and though ostensibly for the purpose of a paper-cutter, it was rarely used as such. Its edges were too sharp to cut paper satisfactorily, and, moreover, it was a wicked-looking affair, and many people had shuddered as they touched it. It had a history, too, and Richard Van Norman used to tell his guests of dark deeds in which the dagger had taken part while it was still in Italy.

Madeleine herself had had a horror of the weapon, though she had often admitted the fascination of its marvellous workmanship, and had said upon several occasions that the thing fairly hypnotized her, and some day she should kill herself or somebody else with it.

From an instinctive sense of duty, Marie started forward, as if to help her mistress, then with a convulsive shudder she screamed again and clasped her hands before her eyes to shut out the awful sight.
===Chinese===
可旁的证据毕竟是有的。范·诺曼小姐身上仍穿着那件黄色缎裙，只是漂亮的绣花胸衣已洇染成一片暗红，一股殷红此刻还正沿着曳地长裙光亮耀眼的裙幅，蜿蜒蔓延而下。

桌上，那只摊开的白手近旁，躺着一把威尼斯匕首。在场之人没有不认得这把匕首的。它多年来一直摆在书房桌上，名义上是把裁纸刀，却难得真派这个用场。它的刃口太过锋利，裁纸反而不甚顺手；况且这物件生就一副凶相，许多人碰它一碰都要打个寒噤。它还有一段来历——理查德·范·诺曼生前常对客人们讲起，这把匕首还在意大利的时候，曾参与过怎样的黑暗勾当。

马德琳自己对这件兵器怕得厉害，虽说她也常承认，那精妙绝伦的做工着实叫她着迷，还不止一次说过，这东西简直像给她施了催眠术，早晚有一天，她要拿它不是杀了自己，就是杀了别人。

出于一种本能的职责感，玛丽抢上一步，仿佛要去救助女主人，随即却浑身一阵痉挛似的战栗，又尖叫一声，双手掩在眼前，不想再看那可怕的景象。

===Original===
Cicely, too, moved slowly toward the silent figure, then turned and again gazed steadfastly at Schuyler Carleton.

There must have been interrogation in her eyes, for the man pointed toward the table, and Cicely looked again, to notice there a bit of paper with writing on it.

She made no motion toward it, but the expression on her face changed to one of bewildered surprise. Before she had time to speak, however, the other people of the house all at once began to gather in the hall.
===Chinese===
西塞莉也缓缓朝那寂然无声的身影挪近，随即又转过身来，再度死死盯住斯凯勒·卡尔顿。

她眼里想必含着诘问，因为那男人朝桌子指了指；西塞莉再看去，才注意到那里有一张写着字的纸片。

她没有朝那纸片挪动半分，脸上的神情却化作一片迷乱的惊愕。她还来不及开口，宅中其他人便忽地一齐聚到了大厅里。

===Original===
Mrs. Markham came first, and though when she saw Madeleine she turned very white and seemed about to faint, she bravely went at once toward the girl, and gently tried to raise the fallen head.

She felt a firm grasp on her shoulder, and turned to see Miss Morton, with a stern, set face, at her side.

“Don’t touch her,” said Miss Morton, in a whisper. “Telephone for a doctor quickly.”

“But she’s dead,” declared Mrs. Markham, at the same time bursting into violent sobs.

“We do not know; we hope not,” went on Miss Morton, and without another word she led Mrs. Markham to a sofa, and sat her down rather suddenly, and then went herself straight to the telephone.

As she reached it she paused only to inquire the name of the family physician.
===Chinese===
最先赶来的是马卡姆太太。她一见马德琳，顿时面无人色，眼看就要晕倒，却仍鼓起勇气径直向姑娘走去，轻轻地想把那颗垂落的头扶起来。

她觉得肩头被一只手牢牢按住，回头一看，莫顿小姐已立在身旁，面色严峻而决然。

“别碰她，”莫顿小姐低声说，“快打电话请医生来。”

“可她已经死了呀，”马卡姆太太一口断言，随即放声痛哭起来。

“还不知道呢；但愿没有，”莫顿小姐接着说。她不再多言，径自把马卡姆太太领到一张沙发上，把她一把按坐下去，自己便大步走向电话机。

走到电话机前，她只停下来问了一句家庭医生姓甚名谁。

===Original===
Harris, the butler, with difficulty articulated the name of Doctor Hills and his telephone number, and without further inquiry Miss Morton called for him.

“Is this Doctor Hills?” she said when her call was answered. “Yes; this is the Van Norman house. Come here at once.﻿ ﻿… No matter; you must come at once﻿—it is very important﻿—a matter of life and death.﻿ ﻿… I am Miss Morton. I am in charge here. Yes, come immediately! Goodbye.”

Miss Morton hung up the receiver and turned to the frightened group of servants.

“You can do nothing,” she said, “and you may as well return to your rooms. Harris may stay, and one of the parlor maids.”

Miss Morton had an imperious air, and instinctively the servants obeyed her.
===Chinese===
管家哈里斯（Harris）结结巴巴、好容易才把希尔斯医生（Doctor Hills）的姓名连同电话号码念清楚。莫顿小姐未再多问一句，当即把电话要了过去。

电话一接通，她便说：“是希尔斯医生吗？是的；这里是范·诺曼宅。请立刻过来。……不必多问；您必须马上来——事情万分紧要——人命关天。……我是莫顿小姐。这里由我主持。是，请即刻就来！再见。”

莫顿小姐挂上听筒，转身对着那群吓坏了的仆人。

“你们帮不上什么忙，”她说，“还是各自回房去吧。哈里斯可以留下，再留一个客厅侍女。”

莫顿小姐自有一股威严气度，仆人们不由自主地听从了她。

===Original===
But Cicely Dupuy was not so ready to accept the dictum of a stranger. She stepped forward and, facing Miss Morton, said quietly, “Mrs. Markham is housekeeper, as well as Miss Van Norman’s chaperon. The servants are accustomed to take their orders from her.”

Miss Morton returned Cicely’s direct gaze. “You see Mrs. Markham,” she said, pointing to the sofa, where that lady had entirely collapsed, and, with her head in a pillow, was shaking with convulsive sobs. “She is for the moment quite incapable of giving orders. As the oldest person present, and as a lifelong friend of Mr. Richard Van Norman, I shall take the liberty of directing affairs in the present crisis.” Then, in a softer tone and with a glance toward Madeleine, Miss Morton continued, “I trust in view of the awfulness of the occasion you will give me your sympathy and cooperation, that we may work in harmony.”

Cicely gave Miss Morton a curious glance that might have meant almost anything, but with a slight inclination of her head she said only, “Yes, madam.”

Then Kitty French and Molly Gardner came downstairs and stood trembling on the threshold.
===Chinese===
西塞莉·杜皮伊却不愿这样轻易听凭一位陌生人的独断。她走上前来，正对着莫顿小姐，平静地说：“马卡姆太太是这宅子的管家，也是范·诺曼小姐的陪伴。仆人们向来听她吩咐惯了。”

莫顿小姐迎住西塞莉逼视的目光。“您瞧马卡姆太太这副样子，”她说着，指向那张沙发——那位太太已经全然支持不住，头埋在靠垫里，抽抽噎噎地抖个不停。“她此刻全然无法发号施令。我是眼下在场年纪最长的人，又是理查德·范·诺曼先生的终身老友，值此危难之际，恕我擅专，暂且主持这里的事务。”随后莫顿小姐语气放缓，朝马德琳那边望了一眼，又道：“今日之事如此惨痛，我相信诸位自会予我同情与合作，好教我们同心协力，共渡此关。”

西塞莉朝莫顿小姐投去好奇的一瞥，那一瞥几乎什么意思都可以是；但她只是微微欠了欠头，说：“是，夫人。”

随后基蒂·弗伦奇和莫莉·加德纳也下了楼，站在门槛上直打颤。

===Original===
“What is it?” whispered Kitty. “What’s the matter with Madeleine?”

“Something dreadful has happened,” said Miss Morton, meeting them at the door. “I have telephoned for Doctor Hills and he will be here soon. Until then we can do nothing.”

“But we can try to help Maddy,” exclaimed Kitty, starting toward the still figure by the table. “Oh, is she hurt? I thought she had fainted!”

As the two girls saw the dread sight, Miss Gardner fainted herself, and Miss Morton bade Marie, who stood shivering in the hall, take care of her.

Relieved at having something to do, Marie shook the girl and dashed water in her face until she regained consciousness, the others, meanwhile, paying little attention.

Schuyler Carleton stood leaning against the doorpost, his eyes fixed on Madeleine’s tragic figure, while Kitty French, who had dropped into a chair, sat with her hands tightly clasped, also gazing at the sad picture.
===Chinese===
“出什么事了？”基蒂悄声问，“马德琳怎么了？”

“出了极为可怕的事，”莫顿小姐迎到门口说。“我已经打电话请希尔斯医生了，他很快就到。在那之前，我们什么也做不了。”

“可我们总还能想法子照护玛迪呀，”基蒂喊着，抬脚便朝桌边那寂然不动的身影走去。“哦，她受伤了吗？我还只当她晕过去了呢！”

两位姑娘一见那可怖的景象，加德纳小姐自己先晕了过去。莫顿小姐便吩咐立在厅里发抖的玛丽去照看她。

好容易有件事可做，玛丽心下略安，摇着那姑娘，往她脸上泼冷水，直到她醒了过来；其余的人此刻谁也没顾上她们。

斯凯勒·卡尔顿倚着门柱站着，两眼定定地望着马德琳那凄惨的身影；基蒂·弗伦奇跌坐在一把椅子里，双手紧紧交握，也凝望着这幅惨象。

===Original===
Although it seemed hours to those who awaited him, it was but a few moments before the doctor came.

Doctor Hills was a clean-cut, alert-looking young man, and his quick eyes seemed to take in every detail of the scene at a glance.

He went straight to the girl at the table and bent over her. Only the briefest examination was necessary before he said gently, “She is quite dead. She has been stabbed with this dagger. It entered a large blood vessel just over her heart, and she bled to death. Who killed her?”

Even as he spoke his eye fell on the written paper which lay on the table. With one of his habitually quick gestures he snatched it up and read it to himself, while a look of great surprise dawned on his face. Immediately he read it aloud:

> I am wholly miserable, and unless the clouds lift I must end my life. I love S., but he does not love me.
===Chinese===
在等候的人觉来，仿佛已过了好几个钟头，其实不过片刻之间，医生便到了。

希尔斯医生是个眉目干净利落、神气警觉的年轻人，一双敏锐的眼睛仿佛只一扫，便把眼前场面的每个细节都收进了眼底。

他径直走到桌边的姑娘跟前，俯下身去。只极简短地查验了一下，他便轻声说道：“她确已死了。她是被这把匕首刺中的。刀刃从心脏上方一条大血管刺入，她失血而死。是谁杀了她的？”

正说话间，他的目光落在桌上那张写了字的纸上。他以惯常的敏捷，一把抓起来默读了一遍，脸上渐渐露出大吃一惊的神色。随即他高声读了出来：

> 我万分痛苦，若这阴霾不散，我只得结束自己的性命。我爱S.，可他并不爱我。

===Original===
After he finished reading, Doctor Hills stood staring at the paper, and looked utterly perplexed.

“I should have said it was not a suicide,” he declared, “but this message seems to indicate that it is. Is this written in Miss Van Norman’s hand?”

Miss Morton, who stood at the doctor’s side, took the paper and scrutinized it.

“It is,” she said. “Yes, certainly that is Miss Van Norman’s writing. I had a letter from her only a few days ago, and I recognize it perfectly.”
===Chinese===
读完，希尔斯医生拿着那张纸怔怔出神，满脸困惑不解。

“我本该说这不是自杀，”他断言道，“可这张字条看来表明正是自杀。这是范·诺曼小姐的笔迹吗？”

立在医生身旁的莫顿小姐接过纸条，细细端详。

“是的，”她说。“没错，这确实是范·诺曼小姐的字迹。没几天前我刚收到过她一封信，这笔迹我一认便知。”

===Original===
“Let me see it,” said Mrs. Markham, in a determined, though rather timid way. “I am more familiar with Madeleine’s writing than a stranger can possibly be.”

Miss Morton handed the paper to the housekeeper without a word, while the doctor, waiting, wondered why these two women seemed so out of sympathy with each other.

“Yes, it is surely Madeleine’s writing,” agreed Mrs. Markham, her glasses dropping off as her eyes filled with tears.

“Then I suppose she killed herself, poor girl,” said the doctor. “She must have been desperate, indeed, for it was a strong blow that drove the steel in so deeply. Who first discovered her here?”

“I did,” said Schuyler Carleton, stepping forward. His face was almost as white as the dead girl’s, and he was scarcely able to make his voice heard. “I came in with a latchkey, and found her here, just as you see her now.”
===Chinese===
“让我瞧瞧，”马卡姆太太说，语气坚决，却又不免带着怯意。“比起一位外人来，我总该比谁都更熟悉马德琳的字迹。”

莫顿小姐一言不发，把纸条递给管家；医生等在一旁，暗自纳罕，这两位妇人之间怎么仿佛如此隔膜不睦。

“是的，这确凿无疑是马德琳的字迹，”马卡姆太太表示认同，泪水盈眶，眼镜也随之滑落下来。

“那么我想，她是自杀的了，可怜的姑娘，”医生说。“她想必真是绝望到了极点，因为能把钢刃刺得这样深，那用力是相当猛的。是谁最先在这里发现她的？”

“是我，”斯凯勒·卡尔顿应道，跨步上前。他的脸色几乎同那死去的姑娘一样惨白，勉强才把声音发出来。“我用自备的钥匙开了门进来，就发现她在这儿，正像您现在看到的这样。”

===Original===
As Carleton spoke Cicely Dupuy stared at him with that curious expression that seemed to show something more than grief and horror. Her emotional bewilderment was not surprising in view of the awful situation, but her look was a strange one, and for some reason it greatly disconcerted the man.

None of this escaped the notice of Doctor Hills. Looking straight at Carleton, but with a kindly expression replacing the stern look on his face, he went on:

“And when you came in, was Miss Van Norman just as we see her now?”

“Practically,” said Carleton. “I couldn’t believe her dead. And I tried to rouse her. Then I saw the dagger on the floor at her feet﻿—”

“On the floor?” interrupted Doctor Hills.

“Yes,” replied Carleton, whose agitation was increasing, and who had sunk into a chair because of sheer inability to stand. “It was on the floor at her feet﻿—right at her feet. I picked it up, and there was blood on it﻿—there is blood on it﻿—and I laid it on the table. And then I saw the paper﻿—the paper that says she killed herself. And then﻿—and then I turned on the lights and rang the servants’ bells, and Cicely﻿—Miss Dupuy﻿—came, and the others, and﻿—that’s all.”
===Chinese===
卡尔顿说话的当口，西塞莉·杜皮伊一直盯着他瞧，那副奇异的表情，似乎流露出的不止是悲伤与惊骇。处境如此骇人，她心绪纷乱原不足为怪，可她那眼色实在异样，也不知怎的，弄得这个男人大为仓皇失措。

这一切都没能逃过希尔斯医生的注意。他直望着卡尔顿，脸上先前那副严厉的神色换成了和悦，接着说道：

“那么您进来的时候，范·诺曼小姐是不是就像我们现在看见的这副样子？”

“差不多，”卡尔顿说。“我不肯相信她死了，还试图唤醒她。后来我才看见匕首在她脚边的地板上——”

“在地板上？”希尔斯医生插话道。

“是的，”卡尔顿答道；他越发激动，实在站立不住，已经跌坐在一把椅子里。“匕首就在她脚边的地板上——就在她脚边。我把它拾了起来，上面有血——上面现在还有血——我把它放到了桌上。然后我看见了那张纸——那张说她自杀的纸。然后——然后我打开了灯，按响了仆人的铃，西塞莉——杜皮伊小姐——来了，其他人也来了，还有——就这些了。”

===Original===
Schuyler Carleton had with difficulty concluded his narration, and he sat clenching his hands and biting his lips as if at the very limit of his powers of endurance.

Doctor Hills again glanced round the assembly in that quick way of his, and said:

“Did any of you have reason to think Miss Van Norman had any thought of taking her own life?”

For a moment no one spoke, and then Kitty French, who, in a despairing, miserable way, was huddled in the depths of a great armchair, said:

“I have heard Madeleine say that some time she would kill herself with that horrid old dagger. I wish I had stolen it and buried it long ago!”
===Chinese===
斯凯勒·卡尔顿好不容易才讲完这段叙述，他坐在那里双拳紧握，嘴唇紧咬，仿佛已到了忍无可忍的地步。

希尔斯医生又以他那迅捷的方式环视了一下在场众人，说道：

“诸位中间，有谁有什么理由认为，范·诺曼小姐起过自寻短见的念头吗？”

一时间无人作声；随后，基蒂·弗伦奇开了口——她正万念俱灰、悲悲戚戚地蜷在一把大扶手椅的深处：

“我听马德琳说过，早晚有一天，她要用那把可恶的老匕首自杀。我真恨不得早把它偷出去埋了！”

===Original===
Doctor Hills turned to Mrs. Markham. “Did you have any reason to fear this?” he inquired.

“No,” she replied; “and I do not think Madeleine meant she would voluntarily use that dagger. She only meant she had a superstitious dread of the thing.”

“Do you understand her reference to her own unhappiness in this bit of writing?” went on the doctor.

“Yes, I think I do,” said Mrs. Markham in a low voice.

“That is enough for the present,” said the doctor, as if to interrupt further confidences. “Although it is difficult to believe a stab of that nature could be self-inflicted, it is possible, and this communication seems to leave no room for doubt. Now, the law of New Jersey requires that in case of a death not by natural means the county physician shall be summoned, and further proceedings are entirely at his discretion. I shall therefore be obliged to send for Doctor Leonard before disturbing the body in any way. He will probably not arrive in less than an hour or so, and I would advise that you ladies retire. You can of course do nothing to help, and as I shall remain in charge, you may as well get what rest you can during the night.”
===Chinese===
希尔斯医生转向马卡姆太太。“您可有理由担心会发生这样的事？”他问。

“没有，”她答道；“而且我不认为马德琳的意思是说，她会存心去用那把匕首。她不过是说，她对那东西怀着一种迷信的恐惧。”

“她在纸条里提到自己不幸福，这层意思您明白吗？”医生接着问。

“是的，我想我明白。”马卡姆太太低声说。

“眼下问到这里也就够了，”医生说，像是有意截住进一步的私谈。“这样性质的一刀，要说会是自伤，实在难以置信，但也并非没有可能，何况这张字条看来已不容置疑了。是这样：新泽西州（New Jersey）的法律规定，凡属非自然死亡，必须请县医生到场，其后的处置悉听其裁夺。因此，在对遗体稍加挪动之前，我不得不先派人去请伦纳德医生（Doctor Leonard）。他大概没有一个钟头上下到不了这里。我劝各位女士都退下吧。你们自然帮不上什么忙，况且自有我在此照应，诸位不妨能歇息多少就歇息多少，将就这一夜吧。”

===Original===
“I thank you for your consideration, Doctor Hills,” said Mrs. Markham, who seemed to have recovered her calmness, “but I prefer to stay here. I could not rest after this awful shock, and I cannot stay away from Madeleine.”

Kitty French and Molly Gardner, who, clasped in each other’s arms, were shivering with excitement and grief, begged to be allowed to stay, too, but Doctor Hills peremptorily ordered them to go to their rooms. Cicely Dupuy was allowed to stay, as in her position of social secretary she might know much of Madeleine’s private affairs. For the same reason Marie was detained, while Doctor Hills asked her a few questions.

Schuyler Carleton sat rigidly in his chair, as immovable as a statue. This man puzzled Doctor Hills. And yet it was surely shock enough almost to unhinge a man’s brain thus to find his intended bride the night before his wedding.

But Carleton seemed absorbed in emotions other than those of grief. Though his face was impassive, his eyes darted about the room looking at one after another of the shocked and terrified group, returning always to the still figure at the table, and as quickly turning his gaze away, as if the sight were unbearable, as indeed it was.

He seemed like a man stunned with the awfulness of the tragedy, and yet conscious of a care, a responsibility, which he could not shake off.

If, inadvertently, his eyes met those of Miss Dupuy, he shifted his gaze immediately. If by chance he encountered Mrs. Markham’s sad glance, he turned away, unable to bear it. In a word, he was like a man at the limit of his endurance, and seemed veritably on the verge of collapse.
===Chinese===
“多谢您体恤，希尔斯医生，”马卡姆太太说，她似乎已恢复了镇定，“可我宁愿留在这儿。经了这样可怕的打击，我是无论如何歇不下的；再说，我也离不开马德琳。”

基蒂·弗伦奇和莫莉·加德纳彼此搂作一团，又惊又悲，抖个不住，也恳求让她们留下，希尔斯医生却断然命她们回房去。西塞莉·杜皮伊获准留下，因为她身任社交秘书，于马德琳的私事或许知道得不少。也是为了这个缘故，玛丽被留了下来，希尔斯医生还问了她几句话。

斯凯勒·卡尔顿直挺挺地坐在椅中，一动不动，如同一尊雕像。这个人叫希尔斯医生好生困惑。不过话说回来，婚礼前夜竟这样撞见自己的未婚妻，单是这一惊，也尽够叫人神智几乎错乱了。

然而卡尔顿似乎浸沉在与悲伤判然有别的种种情绪之中。他脸上虽毫无表情，一双眼睛却在屋里溜来溜去，把这群惊骇失色的人挨个儿打量，目光总又回到桌边那纹丝不动的身影上，可一碰到便立刻挪开，仿佛那景象令人不堪卒睹——事实上也确乎如此。

他像一个被这桩惨祸的骇人吓懵了的人，心里却又分明压着一桩牵挂、一副担子，怎么也卸不下来。

倘若不留神同杜皮伊小姐的目光撞上，他立刻便把视线挪开；倘若偶然触到马卡姆太太悲戚的一瞥，他便转过脸去，承受不住。总而言之，他像一个已到忍耐极限的人，眼看当真要垮下来了。

## IV. Suicide or —﻿—﻿—? / 四　自杀——还是……？

===Original===
Miss Morton, also, seemed to have distracting thoughts. She sat down on the sofa beside Mrs. Markham, then she jumped up suddenly and started for the door, only to turn about and resume her seat on the sofa. Here she sat for a few moments apparently in deep thought. Then she rose, and slowly stalked from the room and went upstairs.

After a few moments, Marie, the French maid, also rose and silently left the room.

Having concluded it was a case for the county physician, Doctor Hills apparently considered that his personal responsibility was at an end, and he sat quietly awaiting the coming of his colleague.

After a time, Miss Morton returned, and again took her seat on the sofa. She looked excited and a little flurried, but strove to appear calm.

It was a dreadful hour. Only rarely anyone spoke, and though glances sometimes shot from the eyes of one to the eyes of another, each felt his gaze oftenest impelled toward that dread, beautiful figure by the table.

===Chinese===
莫顿小姐似乎也心神不定。她在马卡姆太太身旁的沙发上坐下，忽而蓦地跳起身来往门口走去，走到半路却又折回来，重新落座在沙发上。她坐在那里，像是陷入了深沉的思索。片刻之后，她站起身，迈着迟缓的大步走出房去，上楼去了。

过了片刻，法国女仆玛丽也随之起身，悄然离房。

希尔斯医生断定这案子该归县医生过问，显然觉得自己肩上的责任已经到了头，便安静地坐着，等候同行的到来。

过了一阵，莫顿小姐回来了，又在沙发上坐了下来。她看上去情绪激动，有几分慌乱，却竭力装出镇定的样子。

这是一个骇人的钟头。众人难得开口，虽然目光偶尔从这个人的眼里倏地射向那个人的眼里，每个人却都觉得，自己的视线总是身不由己地被引向桌旁那具美丽而可怖的身躯。

===Original===
At last Schuyler Carleton, with an evident effort, said suddenly, “Oughtn’t we to send for Tom Willard?”

Mrs. Markham gave a start. “Of course we must,” she said. “Poor Tom! He must be told. Who will tell him?”

“I will,” volunteered Miss Morton, and Doctor Hills looked up, amazed at her calm tone. This woman puzzled him, and he could not understand her continued attempts at authority in a household where she was a comparative stranger. And yet might it not be merely a kind consideration for those who were nearer and dearer to the principals of this awful tragedy?

But even as he thought this over, Miss Morton had gone to the telephone, her heavy silk gown rustling as she crossed the room, and her every movement assertive of her own importance.

Calling up the Mapleton Inn, she succeeded, after several attempts, in rousing some of its occupants, and finally was in communication with young Willard himself. She did not tell him of the tragedy, but only asked him to come over to the house at once, as something serious had happened, and returned to her seat with a murmured observation that Tom would arrive as soon as possible.

===Chinese===
终于，斯凯勒·卡尔顿明显费了一番力气，突然开口道：“我们是不是该派人去请汤姆·威拉德？”

马卡姆太太惊得一颤。“当然得请他来，”她说，“可怜的汤姆！这消息总得让他知道。谁去告诉他呢？”

“我去。”莫顿小姐自告奋勇。希尔斯医生闻声抬头，惊异于她语气的平静。这个女人令他大惑不解：她在这户人家里本不过是个近乎外人的宾客，却一再越俎代庖、发号施令。可再一想，这会不会只是出于一片好心，替那些与这桩惨剧的当事人更亲近、更痛楚的人分担些事呢？

可他这边还在思量，莫顿小姐已经走到电话机旁。她穿过房间时，厚实的绸裙窸窣作响，举手投足无不显出一副自命不凡的气派。

她接通梅普尔顿旅馆，试了几回才唤醒旅馆里的人，终于同年轻的威拉德本人通了话。她没有告诉他出了惨事，只说宅里发生了要紧的事情，请他即刻过来；随后便回到座位上，低声说了句汤姆会尽快赶到。

===Original===
Again the little group lapsed into silence. Cicely Dupuy was very nervous, and kept picking at her handkerchief, quite unconscious that she was ruining its delicate lace edge.

Doctor Hills glanced furtively from one to another. Many things puzzled him, but most of all he was at a loss to understand the suicide of this beautiful girl on the very eve of her wedding.

At last Tom Willard came.

Miss Morton met him at the door, and took him into the drawing-room before he could turn toward the library.

Schuyler Carleton’s frantic touches on various electric buttons had turned on all the lights in the drawing-room. As no one had noticed this, the great apartment had remained illuminated as if for a festivity, and the soft, bright lights fell on the floral bower and the elaborate decorations that had been arranged for the wedding day.

===Chinese===
这一小组人重又归于沉默。西塞莉·杜皮伊紧张得厉害，手里不住地揪扯手帕，全然不觉自己已把那精致的花边毁坏了。

希尔斯医生悄悄地逐一打量众人。许多事情都令他费解，而最令他无从索解的是：这位美丽的姑娘，何以竟在婚礼前夜自寻短见。

汤姆·威拉德终于来了。

莫顿小姐在门口迎住他，不等他转身往书房去，便把他领进了大客厅。

斯凯勒·卡尔顿方才发急，在各处电钮上乱按一气，竟把大客厅里的灯全拧亮了。谁也没有留意这件事，于是这间大厅便一直灯火通明，宛如佳期盛会将至，柔和明亮的灯光洒落在花亭上，洒落在为婚礼精心布置的种种装饰上。

===Original===
“What is it?” asked Tom, his own face white with an impending sense of dread as he looked into Miss Morton’s eyes.

As gently as possible, but in her own straightforward and inevitably somewhat abrupt way, Miss Morton told him.

“I want to warn you,” she said, “to prepare for a shock, and I think it kinder to tell you the truth at once. Your cousin Madeleine﻿—Miss Van Norman﻿—has taken her own life.”

“What?” Tom almost shouted the word, and his face showed an absolutely uncomprehending amazement.

“She killed herself tonight,” Miss Morton went on, whose efforts were now directed toward making the young man understand, rather than towards sparing his feelings.

===Chinese===
“出了什么事？”汤姆问。他望着莫顿小姐的眼睛，脸上已因大祸将临的预感而一片惨白。

莫顿小姐尽量放轻语气，却仍旧是她那直来直去、因而也不免有些生硬的做派，把事情对他说了。

“我要提醒你，”她说，“先做好受打击的准备，我想还是即刻把实话告诉你的好。你的表妹马德琳——范·诺曼小姐——结束了自己的性命。”

“什么？”这两个字汤姆几乎是喊出来的，脸上是一副全然不解的惊愕。

“她今夜自杀了。”莫顿小姐接着说。她此刻的心思已不在顾惜对方的感受，而在于让这年轻人明白过来。

===Original===
But Tom could not seem to grasp it. “What do you mean?” he said, catching her by both arms. “Madeleine? Killed herself?”

“Yes,” said Miss Morton, shaken out of her own calm by Tom’s excited voice. “In the library, after we had all gone to bed, she stabbed herself with that horrible paper-cutter thing. Did you know she was unhappy?”

“Unhappy? No; why should she be? Tomorrow was to have been her wedding day!”

“Today,” corrected Miss Morton. “It is already the day on which our dear Madeleine was to have become a bride. And instead﻿—” Glancing around the brilliant room and at the bridal bower, Miss Morton’s composure gave way entirely, and she sobbed hysterically. At this Cicely Dupuy came across from the library. Putting her arm around Miss Morton, she led the sobbing woman away, and without a word to Tom Willard gave him a glance which seemed to say that he must look out for himself, for her duty was to attend Miss Morton.

As the two women left the drawing-room Tom followed them. He walked slowly, and stared about as if uncertain where to go. He paused a moment midway in the room, and, stooping, picked up some small object from the carpet, which he put in his waistcoat pocket.

===Chinese===
可汤姆像是怎么也抓不住这个事实。“你这是什么意思？”他说着，一把抓住她的双臂，“马德琳？自杀了？”

“是的。”莫顿小姐说，汤姆激动的嗓音把她自己的镇定也震乱了。“在书房里，等我们都上床之后，她用那件裁纸刀似的可怕东西刺进了自己的身子。你可知道她过得不快活？”

“不快活？不。她怎么会不快活？明天本该是她成婚的日子呀！”

“是今天。”莫顿小姐纠正道，“我们亲爱的马德琳本该做新娘的日子，已经到了。可如今却——”她环顾这灯火辉煌的客厅和那座新娘花亭，镇定彻底瓦解，歇斯底里地啜泣起来。西塞莉·杜皮伊闻声从书房那边过来，搂住莫顿小姐的胳膊，把这位泣不成声的妇人搀了出去；她对汤姆·威拉德一句话也没说，只递去一瞥，仿佛在说：他得自己照顾自己了，她的本分是照看莫顿小姐。

两位妇人走出大客厅时，汤姆跟在后面。他走得很慢，眼睛四下张望，仿佛不知道该往哪里去。行至房间中央，他停了片刻，弯腰从地毯上拾起一件小东西，放进了马甲口袋。

===Original===
A moment more and he had crossed the hall and stood at the library door, gazing at the scene which had already shocked and saddened the others.

With a groan, as of utter anguish, Tom involuntarily put up one hand before his eyes.

Then, pulling himself together with an effort, he seemed to dash away a tear, and walked into the room, saying almost harshly, “What does it mean?”

Doctor Hills rose to meet him, and by way of a brief explanation he put into Tom’s hand the paper he had found on the table. Tom read the written message, and looked more stupefied than ever. With a sudden gesture he turned towards Schuyler Carleton and said in a low voice, “but you *did* love her, didn’t you?”

“I did,” replied Carleton simply.

===Chinese===
又过片刻，他已穿过大厅，站到书房门口，凝望着那幅早已令旁人震惊哀恸的景象。

汤姆不由自主地抬起一只手掩住双眼，喉间发出一声仿佛痛彻心腑的呻吟。

随后他强自振作，像是抹去了一滴眼泪，走进房来，口气近乎严厉地问道：“这到底是怎么回事？”

希尔斯医生起身迎他，权作简短的说明，把他在桌上发现的那张字条放进汤姆手里。汤姆读了那行字，神色比方才更加呆愕。他猛然转向斯凯勒·卡尔顿，低声说道：“可你确实是爱她的，不是吗？”

“我确实爱她。”卡尔顿简单地答道。

===Original===
“Why should she have thought you didn’t?” went on Tom, looking at the paper, and seeming to soliloquize rather than to address his question to anyone else.

As this was the first time that the “S.” in Madeleine’s note had been openly assumed to stand for Schuyler Carleton, there was a stir of excitement all round the room.

“I don’t know,” said Carleton, but a dull, red flush spread over his white face and his voice trembled.

“You don’t know!” said Tom, in cutting tones. “Man, you *must* know.”

But no reply was made, and, dropping into a chair, Tom buried his face in both hands and remained thus for a long time.

===Chinese===
“那她怎么会以为你不爱她呢？”汤姆接着说，眼睛望着那张字条，与其说是在问旁人，不如说是在自言自语。

马德琳字条上的那个“S”，这是头一回被人公然认定指的就是斯凯勒·卡尔顿，满屋子顿时起了一阵骚动。

“我不知道。”卡尔顿说。可他苍白的脸上泛起一片暗红，嗓音也颤了。

“你不知道！”汤姆语带讥刺地说，“老兄，你必须知道。”

但无人应声。汤姆颓然坐进椅子里，双手掩面，就这样呆了许久。

===Original===
Tom Willard was a large, stout man, and possessed of the genial and merry demeanor which so often accompanies avoirdupois. Save for his occasional, though really rare, bursts of temper, Tom was always in joking and laughing mood.

To see him thus in an agonized, speechless despair deeply affected Mrs. Markham. Tom had always been a favorite with her, and not even Madeleine had regretted more than she the estrangement between Richard Van Norman and his nephew. And even as Mrs. Markham looked at the bowed head of the great strong man she suddenly bethought herself for the first time that Tom was now heir to the Van Norman fortune.

She wondered if he had himself yet realized it; and then she scolded herself for letting such thoughts intrude so unfittingly soon. And yet she well knew that it would not be in ordinary human nature long to ignore the fact of such a sudden change of fortunes. As she looked at Tom her glance strayed toward Mr. Carleton, and then the thought struck her that what Tom had gained this man had lost. For had Madeleine lived the Van Norman money would have been, in a way, at the disposal of her husband. The girl’s death then would make Tom a rich man, while Schuyler Carleton would remain poor. He had always been poor, or at least far from wealthy, and more than one gossip was of the opinion that he had wooed Miss Van Norman not entirely because of disinterested love for her.

While Mrs. Markham was busy with these fast-following thoughts a voice in the doorway made her look up.

A quiet, unimportant-looking man stood there, and was respectfully addressing Doctor Hills.

===Chinese===
汤姆·威拉德生得高大壮实，带着胖人常有的那种和气快活的神气。除了偶尔——实在也难得——发上一阵脾气，汤姆总是嘻嘻哈哈、有说有笑的。

眼见他这般痛楚无言、万念俱灰的模样，马卡姆太太深受触动。汤姆向来最得她的欢心，当年理查德·范·诺曼与侄儿失和一事，就连马德琳的痛惜也未必比她更深。而就在马卡姆太太望着这位魁梧大汉低垂的头时，她突然头一遭想到：汤姆如今已是范·诺曼家产的继承人了。

他自己是否已经意识到了这一点呢？随即她又责备自己，怎能让这种念头在这样不合时宜的时刻钻进脑子。然而她也深知，人的天性本就经不起这般家运骤变，任谁也不会久久无视这个事实。她望着汤姆，目光不由得溜向卡尔顿先生，忽然又生出一个念头：汤姆所得到的，正是此人失去的。因为马德琳倘若在世，范·诺曼家的钱财在某种意义上便尽归她丈夫支配。如今姑娘一死，汤姆就要成为富翁，而斯凯勒·卡尔顿依旧贫穷。他一向清寒，至少远算不上富有，早就有不止一个说闲话的人断言，他向范·诺曼小姐求婚，未必全是出于无私的爱情。

马卡姆太太正被这些接踵而至的念头缠住，门口一个声音使她抬起了头。

门口站着一个神情安静、相貌不起眼的男人，正恭恭敬敬地向希尔斯医生说话。

===Original===
“I’m Hunt, sir,” he said, “a plainclothes man from headquarters.”

The three men in the room gave a start of surprise, and each turned an inquiring look at the newcomer.

“Who sent you? And what for?” asked Doctor Hills.

“I’ve been here all night, sir. I’m on guard in the present room upstairs.”

“I engaged him,” said Mrs. Markham. “Madeleine’s presents are very valuable, and although the jewels are still in the bank, the silver and other things upstairs are worth a large amount, and I thought best to have this man remain here during the night.”

===Chinese===
“我是亨特（Hunt），先生，”他说，“总部派来的便衣。”

屋里三个男人都惊得一怔，各自向这位来客投去探询的目光。

“谁派你来的？来做什么？”希尔斯医生问。

“我整夜都待在这儿，先生。我负责看守楼上那间存放贺礼的屋子。”

“是我雇的他。”马卡姆太太说，“马德琳收的贺礼十分贵重，首饰虽然还存放在银行里，楼上那些银器和其他物件也值一大笔钱，我想夜里最好留个人看守。”

===Original===
“A very wise precaution, Mrs. Markham,” said Doctor Hills; “and why did you leave your post, my man?”

“The butler told me of what had happened, and I wondered if I might be of any service down here. I left the butler in charge of the room while I came down to inquire.”

“Very thoughtful of you,” said Doctor Hills, with a nod of appreciation; “and while I hardly think so, we may have use for you before the night is over. I am expecting Doctor Leonard, the county physician, and until he comes I can do nothing. I am sure the room above is sufficiently guarded for the time being, so suppose you sit down here a few minutes and wait.”

Mr. Hunt chose to take a seat in the hall, just outside the library door, and thus added one more solemn presence to the quietly waiting group.

And now Doctor Hills had occasion to add another puzzling condition to those that had already confronted him.

===Chinese===
“这个防备很明智，马卡姆太太，”希尔斯医生说，“那么你为什么离开岗位呢，伙计？”

“管家把出的事告诉了我，我想楼下或许用得着我。下来问询之前，我已托管家代为看守那间屋子。”

“你想得很周到。”希尔斯医生点头称许道，“虽说多半用不着，今夜过去之前，也难说有没有借重你的地方。我正等着县医生伦纳德过来，他不来，我什么都不能做。眼下楼上那间屋子想必看守妥当，你不如就在这儿坐下等几分钟。”

亨特先生却在门厅里拣了个座，正对着书房门外坐下了，于是又给这群静静等候的人平添了一分肃穆的气象。

而这时，希尔斯医生又眼见一桩新的蹊跷，加在了先前摆在眼前的诸般疑团之上。

===Original===
Almost everyone in the room was curiously affected by the appearance of this detective, or plainclothes man, as he was called.

Schuyler Carleton gave a start, and his pale face became whiter yet.

Cicely Dupuy looked at him, and then turning her glance toward Mr. Hunt, whom she could see through the doorway, she favored the latter with a stare of such venomous hatred that Doctor Hills with difficulty repressed an exclamation.

Cicely’s big blue eyes roved from Hunt to Carleton and back again, and her little hands clenched as with a firm resolve of some sort in her mind; she seemed to brace herself for action.

Her hovering glances annoyed Carleton; he grew nervous and at last stared straight at her, when her own eyes dropped, and she blushed rosy red.

===Chinese===
屋里几乎每个人，见了这位侦探——或者照他们的说法，便衣——露面，都异样地变了神色。

斯凯勒·卡尔顿猛地一惊，苍白的脸愈发没了血色。

西塞莉·杜皮伊望了望他，又把目光转向透过门口望得见的亨特先生，朝后者投去一瞥，那目光怨毒恨切，令希尔斯医生好不容易才咽下一声惊呼。

西塞莉那双蓝色大眼睛在亨特与卡尔顿之间来回逡巡，一双小手紧紧攥起，仿佛心里已打定了什么主意；她像是在振作精神，预备有所行动。

她这游移不定的目光惹恼了卡尔顿；他渐渐焦躁起来，末了索性直勾勾地瞪住她，她的眼睛这才垂了下去，脸上涨起玫瑰般的红晕。

===Original===
But this side-play was observed by no one but Doctor Hills, for the others were evidently absorbed in serious thoughts of their own concerning the advent of Mr. Hunt.

Tom Willard stared at him in a sort of perplexity; but Tom’s good-natured face had worn that perplexed look ever since he had heard the awful news. He seemed unable to understand, or even to grasp the facts so clearly visible before him.

But Miss Morton was more disturbed than anyone else. She looked at Hunt, and an expression of fear came into her eyes. She fidgeted about, she felt in her pocket, she changed her seat twice, and she repeatedly asked Doctor Hills if he thought Doctor Leonard would arrive soon.

Doctor Leonard did not live in Mapleton, but motored over from his home in a nearby village. He was a stranger to all those awaiting him in the Van Norman house, with the exception of Doctor Hills. Unlike that pleasant-mannered young man, Doctor Leonard was middle aged, of a crusty disposition and curt speech.

When he came, Doctor Hills presented him to the ladies, and before he had time to introduce the two men, Doctor Leonard said crossly, “Put the women out. I cannot conduct this affair with petticoats and hysterics around me.”

===Chinese===
不过这出小小的戏码，只有希尔斯医生一人看在眼里，其余的人显然都各自埋头，思量着亨特先生到来这件事。

汤姆·威拉德满脸困惑地望着他；其实自打听到那个可怕的消息，汤姆那张厚道的脸就一直挂着这副茫然的神情。他似乎理解不了，甚至连明摆在眼前的事实也把握不住。

而心神最不宁的，还得数莫顿小姐。她望着亨特，眼里露出惧意。她坐立不安，一会儿伸手进口袋掏摸，一会儿两度挪换座位，还再三问希尔斯医生，伦纳德医生是否很快就到。

伦纳德医生并不住在梅普尔顿，是从邻近村子的家里开汽车赶来的。凡在范·诺曼宅中候他的人，他一概不认识，只有希尔斯医生除外。与那位举止温和的年轻人不同，伦纳德医生人到中年，性情乖戾，言语唐突。

他一到，希尔斯医生便引他与几位女宾相见；两位男士还来不及引见，伦纳德医生已不耐烦地说道：“把女人们都请出去。身边围着裙钗和歇斯底里的哭闹，我没法办这件差事。”

===Original===
Though not meant to reach the ears of the ladies, the speech was fairly audible, and with a trace of indignation Miss Morton arose and left the room. Mrs. Markham followed her, and Cicely went also.

Doctor Leonard closed the library doors, and, turning to Doctor Hills, asked for a concise statement of what had happened.

In his straightforward manner Doctor Hills gave him a brief outline of the case, including all the necessary details.

“And yet,” he concluded, “even in the face of that written message, I cannot think it a suicide.”

“Of course it’s a suicide,” declared Doctor Leonard in his blustering way; “there is no question whatever. That written confession which you all declare to be in her handwriting is ample proof that the girl killed herself. Of course you had to send for me﻿—the stupid old laws of New Jersey make it imperative that I shall be dragged out many miles away from my home for every death that isn’t in conventional deathbed fashion; but there is no suspicion of foul play here. The poor girl chose to kill herself, and she has done so with the means which she found near at hand. I will write the burial certificate and leave it with you. There is no occasion for the coroner.”

===Chinese===
这话本没打算让女宾们听见，却听得相当真切。莫顿小姐带着几分愠色，起身离房。马卡姆太太随她出去，西塞莉也走了。

伦纳德医生关上书房的门，转向希尔斯医生，要他把事情经过简明扼要地讲一遍。

希尔斯医生以他一贯直截了当的方式，把案情的梗概讲了一遍，凡属必要的细节，一点不落。

“只是，”他末了说道，“哪怕那张亲笔字条就摆在眼前，我也无法相信这是自杀。”

“当然是自杀。”伦纳德医生以他那粗声大气的做派断然宣称，“这里头毫无疑义。那份你们众口一词说出自她亲笔的绝笔书，便是姑娘自戕的充分凭据。你们自然非把我请来不可——新泽西州那些愚蠢的老法律定了死规矩，凡不是规规矩矩死在病榻上的，都得把我从许多英里外的家里拖来一趟；可这儿没有半点他杀的嫌疑。可怜的姑娘要寻死，就用了她顺手找得到的家什，如此而已。我来开下葬证明，留在你这儿。用不着惊动验尸官。”

===Original===
“Thank God for that!” exclaimed Schuyler Carleton, in a fervent tone.

“Amen,” said Tom. “It’s dreadful enough to think of poor Maddy as she is, but had it been anyone else who﻿—”

Unheeding the ejaculations of the two men, Doctor Hills said earnestly, “But, Doctor, if it had not been for the written paper, would you have called it suicide?”

“That has nothing to do with the case,” declared Doctor Leonard testily. “The paper is there, and is authentic. No sane man could doubt that it is a suicide after that.”

“But, Doctor Leonard, it would seem impossible for a woman to stab herself at that angle, and with such an astonishing degree of force; also to pull the dagger from the wound, cast it on the floor, and then to place her arm in that particular position on the table.”

===Chinese===
“这就谢天谢地了！”斯凯勒·卡尔顿热切地呼道。

“阿门。”汤姆说，“可怜的玛迪落到这步田地，想想已经够惨的了；倘若是别人下的手——”

希尔斯医生顾不上理会这两个人的感叹，恳切地说：“可是，医生，倘若没有那张字条，您还会断为自杀吗？”

“那与案情无关。”伦纳德医生不耐烦地宣称，“字条就在那里，真迹无疑。有此为凭，凡是神志清明的人，都不会怀疑这是自杀。”

“可是，伦纳德医生，一个女子似乎不可能以那样的角度、用那样惊人的力道刺进自己身子；更不可能把匕首从伤口拔出来，掷在地上，再把自己的手臂在桌上摆成那么个特别的姿势。”

===Original===
“Why do you say in that particular position?”

“Because the position of her right arm is as if thrown there carelessly, and not as if flung there in a death agony.”

“You are imaginative, Doctor Hills. The facts may not seem possible, but since they are the facts you must admit that they are possible.”

“Very well, Doctor Leonard, I accept your decision, and I relinquish all professional responsibility in the matter.”

“You may do so. There is no occasion for mystery or question. It is a sad affair, indeed, but no crime is indicated beyond that of self-destruction. The written confession hints at the motive for the deed, but that is outside my jurisdiction. Who is the man in the hall? I fancied him a detective.”

===Chinese===
“你为什么说是那么个特别的姿势？”

“因为她右臂的姿势，像是漫不经心搁在那里的，不像是临死的剧痛中猛然甩上去的。”

“你的想象力太丰富了，希尔斯医生。这些事实看上去或许不可能，可既然它们就是事实，你就得承认它们是可能的。”

“很好，伦纳德医生，我遵从您的裁断，并就此卸去我在此事上的一切职业责任。”

“你尽管卸去。此事无须故弄玄虚，也无须再行查问。这确是一桩伤心事，但除自戕之外，看不出任何别的罪名。那份绝笔书暗示了此事的缘由，可那不归我管。门厅里那个人是谁？我瞧他像个侦探。”

===Original===
“He is; that is, he is a man from headquarters who is here to watch over the bridal gifts. He came downstairs thinking we might require his services in another way.”
